mr president, i would like to thank you. the spring meeting each year was to be devoted to economic and social issues, to growth and development. it was very little this year. i do not deny that there were important subjects to discuss, but i would like to protest against the fact that economic and social development was almost completely neglected. the lisbon process is not working at all as it was intended and the problems are great. unemployment is high, investment is not coming in, and so on and so forth. of course, strong policy efforts would be needed in the member states, as this is where economic policy must be concretely formulated. instead, the only guidelines that come from europe with real force are the stability pact, which, on the contrary, imposes a straitjacket on them. i fully share romano prodi's view that the stability pact is idiotic. it is time to do something about it and to give a little more dynamism to economic development in europe. the social dimension of the lisbon process is also not functioning as it should. everyone talks about lifelong learning, but in reality the member states are not investing in education at all. they're cutting back. there's a real need for something, and the voters are responding. we see in germany and france how the electorate reacts to economic and social policies which are now being allowed to run their own course.
mr president, mr president of the commission, mr president of the council! the events in madrid have put the fight against crime and terrorism at the top of the european agenda. it is a european effort of a new dimension that is needed. european leaders should have realised long before the madrid attack, and preferably before the treaty of amsterdam, that one of their most important political responsibilities is to guarantee the union's external and internal security. the achievement of sufficiently effective and multifaceted measures requires respect for fundamental rights, but also democratic transparency and control. otherwise, the measures with direct effect on citizens risk becoming too weak and too weakly anchored. this is especially true of intelligence. eu leaders should have given the eu more real powers to tackle terrorism and crossborder crime long before it is now forced into panic measures. years of inactivity are reflected in the programme which the irish presidency is now setting before us in an exemplary manner, but the issues deserve a long-term approach. the stark reality is that eu leaders should have shown european political leadership long before. the commission is the key actor. it would be natural to build on the expertise we already have in the commission and to complement it with a commissioner who fills the gap that the expanded fight against terrorism risks creating in the basic protection of citizens, not least the protection of privacy. the crisis package is not enough to combat terrorism; long-term action on a community basis is needed.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. counterterrorism and protecting privacy are not mutually exclusive. the long-term and effective fight against terrorism requires respect for fundamental rights. the us requirements have put airlines in an impossible situation, as the requirements are a violation of eu privacy protection or are a hindrance to aviation. the commission has tried to find a solution in an exemplary manner. we therefore do not support the demands in the resolution that the commission should withdraw the proposal. the proposal improves the present situation but still suffers from serious shortcomings. the data protection officers of the member states and the eu, the article 29 group, point to the worrying lack of data protection. the proposal requires a thorough judicial review, which is why we support the case being examined by the court of justice. an immediate switch from the 'pull' to the 'push' system is necessary to correct the lack of data protection. the conditions for a satisfactory agreement are that passengers are guaranteed information on what data is being transferred to the us authorities and that passengers are in practice able to voluntarily agree to such transfer of personal and financial data and to review the data recorded.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. the fight against terrorism must be global and preventive, but it must always be balanced against respect for the privacy of the individual. the european parliament voted today on an eu-us agreement on the transfer of data not selected on a clear basis, from passenger lists to us customs and border control authorities. we object to the extent of the pnr data to which the united states will have access, the number of authorities that have access to it, the intended use of the data, the lack of appeal for those who are denied entry to the united states on the basis of the pnr data and the direct access of the united states to the data without having to ask for it. there is currently no legal basis in the eu for using the commercial data in pnr for the protection of public security. we therefore propose instead that a genuine international agreement be drawn up between the eu and the usa, which clearly lays down objectives, data, guarantees and responsibilities. the agreement in its present form threatens to undermine the rule of law. that is why we support mrs boogerd-quaak's report and vote against this agreement with the united states.
(parliament adopted the legislative resolution) terrorism is one of the greatest scourges of our time and must be fought vigorously and effectively at both national and international level. international cooperation in the fight against terrorism must include personal data, in particular in the context of aviation, in order to be effective. we support the collection and transfer of data on air passengers to the us authorities, but this must be done on the basis of clear legal rules (in the us and europe), be proportionate to the objective and there must be clear rules on what data is collected. this must not be done automatically (the so-called 'pull' system). the authorities that have access to the data must be clearly identified and the period for which the data are stored must be clearly specified. the responsibility of airlines towards passengers must be established and passengers must be able to appeal to an independent body. it is regrettable that personal data of european passengers are being transferred to the united states without a legal basis, as this is a violation of the protection of privacy. we must do something about these irregularities. the commission should therefore withdraw the proposal and submit a new legislative proposal which takes particular account of the above points. only legislation of this kind can reconcile the fight against terrorism with respect for privacy and fundamental rights. we have voted in favour of paragraphs 1 and 10 of the motion for a resolution (adopted by 226 votes to 203 and 233 to 207).
in the case of the european parliament, the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european parliament is able to exercise its right of initiative in the matter of health and safety at work. we moderates voted against the above report because we do not believe that local environmental issues - in this case, for example, indoor air quality and passive smoking - belong at eu level. the principle of subsidiarity should also be applied to decisions on the protection of local water resources.
mr president, mr commissioner, mr minister, i would like to thank you for your work. i would like to begin by thanking alima boumediene-thiery for her excellent work on an important report. what is the situation in the eu-15, which is shaping a union based on the equal value of human beings, humanism, tolerance and human dignity? i think that most of us here can see the cracks in the european union's façade. this is our union: refugees are abused, marginalized and risk losing their lives on their way to a better life in europe. an important step towards a common refugee policy was taken yesterday, as the minister said, but it has taken a long time and the proposals are not far enough. 800,000 women and children are bought and sold as commodities in a despicable sex trade. women are being gender-disfigured, at risk of forced marriage and subjected to daily violence simply because they are women. freedom of the media and of expression is restricted. homosexuals are discriminated against. anti-semitism is on the rise, and jews are hearing the same slogans that were heard across europe in the 1930s. the report presented today is a clear example of hatred and violence. racism and intolerance against immigrants are spreading. millions of immigrants are excluded from society. muslims are being harassed. islamic fundamentalism is growing strong in the suburbs of our cities. yes, it is in our union that the lives of 190 ordinary spaniards ended on a perfectly ordinary day on their way to work. we can say that freedom has never been stronger than this spring, when europe is uniting and healing old wounds, but at the same time we know that our eu-15 is carrying many shortcomings into the new eu on 1 may 2004. i would like to conclude by saying that it is unfortunate that almost all member states are imposing restrictions on the new member states. this could have been avoided by eu leaders acting together, by being better prepared and adapting the rules in time. many of our new friends are rightly disappointed by this. i would like to conclude by saying that the eldr group is asking the house to support two amendments on the european arrest warrant and the french veil ban.
mr president, i would like to thank you. this is the second time today that we've discussed mining. this morning it was jonas sjöstedt's report and now the mining industry is indirectly affected by this resolution. i agree with both minister roche and commissioner vitorino that the energy and mining industries are of great importance to the economies of many poor countries. we also know that this type of industrial activity is often carried out in ways which have not directly benefited the poor majority, which have often led to serious damage to the environment and which have also unfortunately led to the fact that the proceeds from the concessions granted have mainly ended up in the pockets of individual ministers and not in the treasury, where they could have contributed to the development of the countries. the study to which we refer here is interesting and important. it shows the opportunities for these industries, but also the risks of not being surrounded by social, environmental and human rights criteria. the consequences are often increased poverty and environmental destruction. we met mr. salim a few weeks ago when he was passing through. he gave a large number of examples of the difficulties faced by the local population in different project areas: forced displacement, environmental damage, etc. it is important that the world bank addresses the criticisms made in the study and i note that both the president of the council and the commission agree with many of the recommendations. it is very important that the eu member states, whose governments make up a large part of the bank's board of directors, coordinate their activities in this area and ensure that the bank follows up the recommendations. i believe that the presidency can play a very important role here. if it is important for the world bank to change its mind, it is of course equally important for a number of other financial institutions to change their minds. vitorino referred to the fact that a certain agreement has been reached on the operation of the export credit boards. yes, but so far it's very thin. we need much stricter criteria for their activities, especially with reference to social, environmental and climate contexts. let me give you an example. between 1994 and 2000, the export credit boards in our oecd countries guaranteed investments in new energy production worth about $100 billion. all that activity was fossil-based. not a cent went to energy efficiency or renewable energy. a series of measures are required. without elevating this report and this study to the level of absolute truth, they are nevertheless a good basis for driving and sharpening policy in relation to both poverty issues and sustainable development.
the committee on agriculture and rural development has tabled a number of amendments to the proposal. mr president, commissioner, i would like to thank you for your reply. if you study agriculture in turkey, you find that it is rather poorly developed. there are almost twice as many farmers in turkey as in the eu-15 as a whole. almost half of the workforce in turkey is engaged in agriculture. when turkey becomes a member of the eu, the eu will have a very important task to play in the development of turkish agriculture. the most difficult task is probably to achieve rural development without creating social problems which could otherwise become very serious when agriculture must necessarily be depopulated and people must find other employment. there have been many positive developments in turkey in recent years. in 2000, for example, a new agricultural policy was introduced, which makes it easier for turkey to approach the european union in this area. at the same time, we are at an early stage in discussing membership and it is difficult to make an accurate assessment. i would like to say that agriculture is probably not the biggest problem in turkey. we have similar problems in countries like romania and bulgaria. there are other issues that pose greater problems, such as human rights, democracy and the rule of law. it is important that we all agree that when the copenhagen criteria are met, then it is ok. then we will start negotiations, and then it will be possible for turkey to become a member. i personally think it would be useful to have a muslim but secularised country as a member of the union, as a bridge between the cultures of europe and the arab world. i know that there are parties and groups in this parliament who do not want turkey to become a member. i think it is important that our majority declare that we welcome turkey, but we must put pressure on it to carry out the reforms that are necessary before it can become a member.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it's a modern form of colonialism. we need a real reform of fisheries policy and a complete change of approach to our relations with the poor countries. we have therefore voted against the report.
- (sv) the swedish christian democrats want the question of the proposed constitution for europe to be resolved in a way that meets the following criteria: (1) that the eu does not have the right to tax our incomes and pensions; (2) that the rotating presidency of the council continues and that no new, centralistic office of eu president is created; (3) that europe's biblical and judeo-christian heritage is recognised in the preamble as a historical fact with regard to our spiritual heritage, in the same way that humanism is specified as the only named philosophical current; (4) that article 51 is maintained with its content, that the eu must conduct a regular dialogue with churches and communities and other religious associations; (5) that each member state has the right to a commissioner with full voting rights; (6) that defence issues are also dealt with in the future on the basis of unanimity on the principle of unanimity; (7) that the rubber clauses on the mechanism of transition pass. it is proposed that they should give the heads of state or government in the council the right to extend the eu's legislative powers by unanimity at the expense of national parliaments and to abandon the right of veto/unanimity in favour of majority decisions - without requiring the approval of national parliaments.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the death penalty is not used in the member states. this is a matter for each country to decide. paragraph 147: we believe that it is for the member states to decide whether they need to make use of transitional rules or not. many member states have opted for transitional arrangements to allow time to prepare for a smooth transition to full freedom of movement. we believe it is important that the transitional rules should be as short as possible.

the rapporteur. thank you, mr president! i understand that the conference of presidents has decided to withdraw this report, which we have previously discussed in the committee on women's rights. this was done at the request of the pse and the ppe, which had made the dishonest argument that the report should be referred back to committee. the committee has had its last meeting and, since i am not running again, deportation would mean the death of this own-initiative report. the report deals with the demand side of the issue, including trafficking in human beings and sexual exploitation. if this parliament does not discuss the report and put that part in its context, i do not see why we should discuss so-called sexual exploitation at all. i therefore ask that the report be put back on the agenda as originally planned under rule 111 of the rules of procedure.
commission. mr president, the commission very much welcomes this debate on company law and corporate governance and the action plan connected therewith – an action plan adopted by the commission almost one year ago. we broadly support the content of the resolution which has been submitted to parliament. we very much thank mrs ghilardotti for the quality of her report. this action plan is based on the firm belief that a self-regulatory market approach, based solely on non-binding recommendations, is clearly not always sufficient to guarantee the adoption of sound corporate governance practices. only if there a number of tailored rules will markets play their disciplining role and ensure diligent business conduct. this is why, in view of the growing integration of european capital markets, a common approach should be adopted at eu level, laying down a few essential rules and adequate coordination of corporate governance codes. recent scandals have therefore strengthened the commission's determination to implement its action plan. my services are actively working in five areas to have proposals ready, where possible, later this year. the five areas are as follows: first, the role of non-executive directors; second, the remuneration of directors; third, the responsibility of directors for company accounts; fourth, the full disclosure of intra-group relations and transactions with related parties, including transparency in the company accounts of offshore special purpose vehicles; and fifth, the disclosure of corporate governance practices. the aim is therefore not to re-examine or reconsider the action plan in the light of recent developments, as requested in the motion for resolution; rather, it is to learn from those developments and to take due account of them when preparing the various initiatives that it contains. when implementing the action plan, the commission will also take due account of the interests of the various stakeholders – to which mrs ghilardotti made reference – and, where appropriate, distinguish between listed and non-listed companies and large and small shareholders, as is also requested in the motion for resolution. it is of the utmost importance that we restore trust and confidence in the audit function. i am therefore pleased to see that the rapporteur agrees with the main elements of the reform which i have set out in the proposal for an eighth directive on statutory audit. that should allow us to proceed quickly and, i hope, reach an agreement between the council and parliament in a single reading. i know that the issue of non-audit services delivered to the audited entity is particularly delicate. the commission has published a recommendation on auditor independence which sets out the conditions under which such services are still allowed. we need to see how this recommendation is applied in practice. many member states are in the process of transposing this recommendation into their national law. it would therefore not be appropriate, at this stage, to fundamentally change our approach in that area. however, in our communication of may 2003, we announced our intention to carry out a study that will examine whether further changes in that area are needed. the results of the study will be available next year. at that time, we shall decide whether new initiatives are necessary. in europe, investors need to be better organised and more vocal advocates of corporate change. however, a sound corporate governance framework will not be achieved by the markets simply acting on their own, nor by the introduction of an overly prescriptive legal infrastructure. the challenge we are all facing here is to find the right balance between regulatory and market-based incentives and penalties. we therefore support the broad thrust of this resolution and we are working resolutely along those lines.
the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. the opinion of the itre committee stresses the need for dynamism and flexibility in corporate governance principles. the principle of subsidiarity should be respected in order to allow for national characteristics and traditions in this area, and we should avoid as far as possible a central management of enterprises. we shall not seek to achieve a harmonization of different codes of corporate governance. voting shares between different shares is another example of something that should not be decided at eu level, as it would create problems for smes in some eu countries. european industry needs even more transparency in its management and accounting. this is beneficial for their future capital supply and market position. i note that the rapporteur's views are in part contrary to the opinion of the committee, but i would point out that the opinion was adopted by a large majority and that it is in line with the commission's communication.
commission. madam president, firstly – and you have heard me say this before – we will have to live with the issue of climate change for a very long time – maybe for generations. it will pose a challenge to humanity for generations to come. it is important that the european union should continue to show leadership on this issue. we should continue to demonstrate that we are willing to take action at home and internationally, that we believe that a truly global problem also requires a global solution. we will also have to be cost-efficient. to be credible, we will have to look at the most cost-efficient solutions; this is part of that process, and in doing so we will be able to mobilise all the various groups of stakeholders. this will help us to do so. my thanks for this again go both to the rapporteur and to the whole european parliament. i would like to comment on two points mentioned during the debate. first of all, russia. as has been mentioned, a team of commissioners will be going to russia later this week in order to discuss a number of issues, including many areas of cooperation with russia. we should not wait for russia – that is true – but the 120 countries that have ratified are not waiting; they are actually carrying out actions on the issue of climate change already. however, we should of course keep up the pressure on russia and make it clear to the russians that they hold the key to the entry into force of the kyoto protocol, that what they do is important, and that we are counting on them. therefore we will continue to work on that. the other issue is an important one: the argument about sinks and our position on sinks. again, i am satisfied that our position on sinks is consistent, since in all the negotiations on the kyoto protocol we have remained rather sceptical on sinks – at least until such time as we can monitor and control sinks and count them reliably. that is one of the arguments, the other being the temporary nature of sinks and emission reductions from them. we have also pursued our line in this proposal and i welcome that. these are the arguments. we still have too many uncertainties, and we have to clarify those; but of course they are in the kyoto protocol, and one day i hope that this will also be another opportunity for brazil and other countries. however, as long as we do not have full knowledge, we should be careful. this will be another effective instrument within our climate change policy.
- thank you, commissioner wallström. - i'll see you in a few minutes. this meeting is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.

- the next item is the report (a5-0265/2004) by mr blokland, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection, on the proposal for a european parliament and council directive on batteries and accumulators and used batteries and accumulators.
commission. madam president, i have distributed a list to the secretariat indicating which amendments are acceptable to the commission commission's position on amendments by parliament: see annex.. i will limit myself to commenting on two issues that have been raised during the debate and explain once more why the commission did not propose a ban. as you know, the commission carried out an extended impact assessment in preparing this proposal. it has assessed the sustainability of different policy options, including a substance ban. it is important to recall that in this proposal the commission has maintained a general restriction on the use of mercury in batteries, in line with the existing battery directive. the restrictions in the end-of-life vehicles directive also continue to apply, so the provisions are there. however, on the basis of this impact assessment, a substance ban for other batteries was not chosen for the following reasons: a substance ban can only be considered if viable substitutes exist. at the moment, these substitutes do not exist for all applications. a ban would always be limited in scope and would never be able to cover all hazardous batteries. as we have already heard, a substance ban might not cover cordless power tools, emergency lighting and industrial applications. in fact, a ban would only represent around 16% of the total nickel cadmium battery market and less than 6% of the total lead battery market. a large percentage of these hazardous batteries are currently in use or stored at home. it is estimated that 60% of rechargeable portable batteries are 'hoarded' or stored at home. they would not be covered by the ban and would still risk ending up in the environment. for cadmium batteries, the substitutes have a shorter lifespan and are more expensive, so that would create more waste and extra costs for consumers. these are the main arguments as to why we chose a closed-loop solution. we have proposed this closed-loop system for all batteries. that should ensure that batteries no longer end up in the waste stream but are collected and recycled instead. for industrial nicad batteries the closed-loop system is guaranteed by the proposed ban on landfill and incineration. those are our main arguments. i suppose that this will be debated from now on, but we all seem to agree that it is important to monitor what is going on and find the best solution to make sure that these hazardous substances and batteries do not end up in the waste stream. they must be taken care of. i thank you for your work on this report and for the debate.
- thank you, commissioner wallström. - i'll see you in a few minutes. this meeting is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
- the next item is the report (a5-0261/2004) by mr wijkman, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament on integrated product policy - life cycle environmental impacts.
the rapporteur. president. - the debate is closed. i'm not going to lie. environmental policy has so far been relatively successful in dealing with point emissions from factories, power stations, etc. the situation is different for non-point emissions, i.e. impacts from the consumption of goods and services. the problem we face is that the accumulated pollution in society, not least through our consumption habits, is constantly increasing. the european environment agency has recently shown that the volume of waste in the 15 member states has increased by 15% since 1990. so even though we've reduced pollution and used energy and materials more efficiently, the gains are being eaten up quickly by increased growth. with global gdp expected to triple or quadruple in the next 30 to 40 years, we must do something radical if we are not to be literally drowned in an ever-growing mountain of garbage and pollution. the integrated product policy we are discussing here today offers excellent opportunities to create a framework of incentives to get us to leave behind production and consumption patterns. these are characterised by a linear flow of waste and wear approach in favour of resource efficiency, waste minimisation, controlled use of hazardous substances and a strengthening of the natural resource base. however, for this to happen, the integrated product policy must be based on a long-term vision of how to change our industrial production model. i mean, what we've learned is that it's not enough to pollute a little less. we must strive for production and consumption patterns that are pure in themselves. the advantage of an integrated product policy is that it is a holistic approach, i.e. it addresses the entire product chain, from the extraction of raw materials through product design and the production phase itself to the stage when the product has been used up. the commission's proposal has a number of good points, but it is still not proactive enough. it seems that the commission has partly abandoned the integrated product policy in favour of the thematic strategies. i don't see it as an either or without as a both. we need the thematic strategies, but we also need an integrated product policy framework to keep the policy together and to be a driving force in the implementation itself. in general, environmental policy, and energy policy too, must be much more coherent and consistent. we have a plethora of directives and regulations today, but too little attention is paid to the whole. the commission's proposal calls for the use of voluntary methods as far as possible. emas and iso are of course good, but research shows that they are not enough. the voluntary nature of the commission's tapping is also linked to market forces, i.e. the operation of the market. however, as environmental costs are rarely factored into prices, the market is not functioning satisfactorily. companies must be given direct incentives, i.e. be able to make money from developing environmentally friendly products. this is rare today, and how can the market help? the commission's communication refers to improving consumer information. that is good, but it is naive to think that better labelling and better environmental information will solve this problem. i have been involved in developing eco-labels in the nordic countries and i know how difficult it is to achieve a wide impact. in my view, we need a dynamic framework for integrated product policy which provides guidelines for the policy as a whole and which supports the thematic strategies. this framework should be characterised by a clear vision which includes the following: first, a systems approach where life cycle thinking is the very basis. secondly, a deeper understanding of how nature and the ecosystem work in order to adapt the product and business models of companies to biological principles. thirdly, the optimisation of the product design itself by choosing materials with the least possible environmental impact. fourthly, the principle that products which have been used up must be separated into their constituent parts and reused, or, alternatively, in the case of biodegradable materials, incorporated into the natural cycle. finally, the information revolution should be exploited to the fullest to promote greater efficiency in the use of energy, materials and dematerialisation. particularly important is to be aware of the opportunities offered by learning from nature and copying nature. i don't have time to go into it now, but there are a number of examples where companies today are taking advantage of these principles and are producing completely clean solutions and making money on top of it. madam president, during the debate in committee the commission representative said that the primary focus was not on a framework directive. i'm sorry about this. i am convinced that this is necessary and i hope that the commission, under the leadership of mrs wallström, will reflect on this and realise that it is important in the long term to create a horizontal policy framework which will help in the overall environmental work.
the commission. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your report. the parliament's draft report supports a life-cycle approach and provides a good guide for how the commission will develop the integrated product policy in the coming years. we've put a lot of effort into finding a name that's better and that can tell the unemployed what this is about. as long as it's an important mission. i really appreciate the efforts behind this report, in particular the work done by the rapporteur, mr anders wijkman. in the commission's communication we stressed the need to show how this could work in practice and we also said that we wanted to use two methods for this. firstly, to carry out a couple of pilot projects which bring together different actors to discuss and, ideally, also implement measures to reduce the environmental impact of these particular products. secondly, we are in the process of drawing up a european list of products with the greatest potential for environmental improvement. such a list can help us to focus our efforts on the most important products. the report contains many interesting insights. let me comment on a few of them, which are important and show the link between the thematic strategies and this proposal, which the rapporteur also mentioned. i would also like to describe my view of the link between the integrated product policy and the other initiatives mentioned in the report, in particular the two thematic strategies: the strategy for the sustainable use of natural resources and the strategy for waste prevention and recycling. my long-term vision for these three areas is actually quite straightforward. i believe that the natural resources strategy should address the general problems of how to break the link between economic growth and environmental impact. the strategy should set out what is to be done to achieve this decoupling and set general objectives on how to protect natural resources against overexploitation and pollution. integrated product policy then aims to transform these resource-oriented objectives - remember that natural resources provide us with minerals, food, clean air and water - into concrete actions to reduce the environmental impact of products and services while creating business opportunities. it does not matter that we start to develop an integrated product policy even before the resource strategy is completed. as we have said in our proposal, we can do a lot already. i also believe that the strategy on waste and recycling should be more clearly focused on the environmental and market problems that arise in that part of the life cycle. it does not mean that the whole life cycle of products is forgotten. on the contrary, the measures taken here must take account of the environmental impact throughout the life cycle of the product, through the application of an integrated product policy. i therefore also do not think that integrated product policy should include resource-based objectives. i think they fit better into the resource strategy and i also do not think that in integrated product policy one should advocate the use of certain materials, whether they are bio-based materials, composites or other, because then one risks ignoring the life cycle perspective. secondly, i would like to emphasize that i do not think this is the right time to make a framework directive on integrated product policy. aside from the question of what can realistically be included in such a directive - it should cover a very large number of tools and concepts while being able to monitor compliance - i simply think it is too early to decide whether such a framework directive is necessary. as impatient as we are, i think we have to wait a little and see how the various players will put an integrated product policy into practice. this applies to industry, governments and consumers alike. if they do not take advantage of the opportunities it offers, we can consider a less voluntary approach, but i think it is too early to draw conclusions on that today. in the course of 2007, the commission will review the two points i have mentioned and the whole of the integrated product policy. if it proves necessary, we will make changes. some examples could be to put more emphasis on services, to advocate a framework directive or to change the integrated product policy as a result of the discussions on the thematic strategies. i think that the rapporteur has made a number of important points in his speech which should definitely guide our thinking more broadly in the future.
- this meeting is adjourned. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
- the next item is the recommendation for second reading (a5-0234/2004) by the committee on employment and social affairs, on the common position of the council with a view to the adoption of a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on the coordination of social security systems (15577/6/2003 - c5-0043/2004 - 1998/0360 (cod)) (rapporteur: mr lambert).
the commission. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i am very pleased that substantial progress has been made in both the council and parliament on this proposal and that we are now reaching the final stage of adoption. the aim of formal approval by the council and parliament before 1 may is of the utmost importance for a number of reasons. most importantly, it will send a strong message to the citizens of both the current and the new member states, showing that the european union has both the will and the capacity to set the right conditions for genuine free movement of persons, the cornerstone of which is social security coordination. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs lambert, on her really excellent report on such a technically difficult subject as the coordination of social security systems. i would also like to thank the committee on employment and social affairs for its work. as you know, the european commission, in the amended proposal it submitted last october, has taken account in whole or in part of most of the amendments adopted by parliament at first reading. the two amendments at second reading clearly improve the quality of the text and can therefore be accepted in their entirety by the commission. i can also assure you that the commission is able to accept some of the other concerns and concerns expressed by some members of the committee on employment and social affairs at second reading in the context of the proposal for a new implementing regulation which the commission will present before the end of this year. and i hope, as mrs lambert said, that by 2006 the regulation and all three annexes will have been adopted. the council's common position is in line with the main objectives of the commission's proposal, which are to simplify and improve the coordination of social security systems for the benefit of european citizens moving within the union. simplification and modernisation of this regulation is a key prerequisite for promoting labour mobility in the union. it is thus closely linked to the overall objective of competitiveness, more and better jobs and social cohesion. it is true that this regulation protects in the field of social security persons who move for any reason, personal or professional, from one member state to another. it is therefore important to the vast majority of european citizens. i shall not list all the improvements which the new rules will bring in comparison with the current regulation, but we can, very shortly, focus our attention on three issues. the new rules will apply to all citizens of the european union covered by the social security system of a member state. the application of the coordination rules to all insured persons will satisfy the need to adapt regulation 1408 to the development of freedom of movement within the union, which is not only a right for workers but also a reality for all european citizens. the new rules will strengthen the principle of equal treatment of european union nationals by introducing the provision on the cross-border recognition of facts and events. this provision is of particular importance for frontier workers. the current regulation does not contain clear provisions on good administration. the new rules fill this gap. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the information provided by the member states. in addition, the institutions will be obliged to reply within a reasonable time to all questions and to provide citizens with all the information necessary to exercise their rights. in conclusion, i would like to say that it has indeed been a long and difficult process which has been successfully completed, and i would like to thank you once again for the painstaking and rapid work you have carried out in your capacity as co-legislator.
commission. madam president, the commission can take on board the concerns of some members of the committee on employment and social affairs in the framework of the proposal for the new implementing regulation. for both of the commission's proposals, for the new implementing regulation and for the content of the three annexes, the parliament will play its role as a co-legislator.
- thank you, chief dimas. this meeting is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon. (the sitting was closed at 11.40 p.m.)
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to begin by thanking the commission and mr luigi cocilovo for the very good and exemplary cooperation they have shown in this report. the report is important because it touches on the most fundamental aspect of our economy, both at european, national and regional level, namely the proper functioning of transport. it is also very important because it can help us to cope with environmental problems, congestion and noise. the commissioner said that there was concern in the council about competitive disadvantages and there is of course concern about competitive disadvantages, because there are too high charges in certain areas, for example in northern scandinavia where we have long distances both to the european market, but also within the region and within the countries. such a tolling system must not contribute to the difficulty of transport, which is detrimental to the economy and employment. the amendments to article 7 of the report say that lower fees should be allowed. i think it is good that this possibility exists, and when it is to be defined, regional policy aspects must be included because, as i said, it must not mean that transport is made more difficult, so that the economy is made more difficult in certain regions of europe. i have heard these thoughts in the cocilovo report and i am most grateful for them.
in the light of the above, i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the european space agency is a member of the european union. we have also previously supported the commission's green paper on space policy adopted on 9 october 2003, the decision on a harmonised european space strategy adopted on 17 january 2002 and the proposal for an action plan for the implementation of the european space policy adopted on 29 january 2004. we have also supported the measures to implement the galileo project. it is important that the eu's competence in the field of space policy should not be extended and that industrial cooperation in the field of space should be mainly carried out on an intergovernmental basis in accordance with the agreement on the european space agency. the eu's involvement should, in the first instance, cover the field of space research. we reject the increased competence of the european union which is planned to be introduced in the draft constitution of the european union.
in writing. - (sv) we fully support the right of free movement of persons as one of the fundamental freedoms of the internal market. that is why we are voting for the lambert report. it is natural that the eu should decide what is to apply to those union citizens who move from one country to another in order to realise the vision of free movement of labour and of other citizens who are able to provide for themselves. but when the eu moves into areas which are mainly to be handled by the member states, there is an obvious risk of overlapping legislation. when countries want to protect their contribution systems, they resort to discriminatory transitional provisions because the detailed eu provisions do not provide for fully fair, permanent measures. pensions and other benefits earned should be able to be taken with them without difficulty when moving to another eu country, while social benefits (such as child and housing benefits) which are not earned individually should not, in principle, be paid to persons residing in other countries. in the context of the union of 25 now being established, regulation 1408/71 on social security schemes should be revised to better adapt it to the new conditions.
in writing. - (sv) what is it? i have chosen to vote against the committee's amendment no 21, which seeks to abolish the swedish share system where a and b shares have different voting weights. a certain degree of harmonisation of the eu's financial market rules is necessary to strengthen the single market and thus growth. the amendment is going in the wrong direction. the system of a and b shares is open, non-discriminatory and based on voluntary purchase agreements. it allows individuals and institutions to invest risk capital in a company without incurring ownership liability. there is no evidence to support the claim that the differential voting system prevents acquisitions. a more fundamental objection is that it is not the eu that should decide on ownership, question ownership or prevent long-term ownership of a company.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to begin by thanking mr konstantinos hatzidakis for his excellent cooperation as usual. firstly, we need a european cohesion policy. we need a solidarity distribution policy in the enlarged eu to compensate for disparities and to compensate regions far from the major markets. secondly, on the budgetary front, we must provide the resources needed to make the enlargement of the european union a success and to boost growth in europe. we'll use the money well. thirdly, the extreme rural areas in northern sweden and finland are losing their objective 1 status under the proposal and this has to be questioned because the geographical disadvantages are there regardless of what europe looks like. we must ensure that these parts of europe get the support they need. fourthly, the commission must ensure that the outermost regions receive their fair share of the future structural funds regulation.
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i have written the report on the discharge of the european development fund. we have discussed this in committee and we have been very unanimous in our analysis of the developments in the european development fund and we have also been very unanimous in our decision on the report. i have also had very good cooperation with the commission and the court of auditors, and i would like to thank those institutions for that. looking at the court of auditors' statement on the european development fund, it can be said that it approves the expenditure, but only almost. the commission is not prepared to give the green light for direct budget support, which in 2002 accounted for about a quarter of the total. this is important because all plans indicate that in future more and more of the assistance will be given as direct budget support. if these funds are to be used correctly and if we are to have the possibility of monitoring whether the funds are being used correctly or not, we must have a completely different quality of budgetary control and budgetary follow-up in the recipient acp countries. the commission still has a lot of work to do before it can give us this report properly. this is a key element in the future revision of the european development fund. the court is also very critical of the annual declaration by the director-general of the directorate-general responsible. the court considers that he could not do so because of the information he had. this is a criticism which the commission should take up, and i conclude that it is very serious. it indicates that the control system is still not working well enough. when we asked the commission for concrete reports evaluating different projects in different acp countries, it took a very long time to produce these reports. this confirms a number of the concerns we had during this work, namely that the headquarters in brussels does not have sufficient overview of the work of the delegations. if you do not have the reports and cannot give them to us, even though they are on the lists, it is difficult to imagine that you have a proper follow-up in place. we believe that this is something that the committee on budgetary control must continue to examine and look at next year. on the whole, we recommend discharge for the european development fund. i would also like to say something about the other reports, which are also much more controversial than my own in this debate, in particular the reports by michiel van hulten and gabriele stauner. i and the gue/ngl group will not vote for the discharge of the council or the committee of the regions and we believe that the votes on the discharge of these two institutions should have been postponed. in the case of the council, it is mainly a matter of its attitude and its unwillingness to cooperate with parliament. the council has been given a timetable, a series of questions, which it is in practice refusing to answer. these requests for answers are in fact fully in line with the existing financial regulation. the council is referring instead to a kind of gentlemen's agreement from the 1970s, which is completely outdated. instead, the council of ministers has graciously chosen to answer certain selected questions to a few selected members, and this is done without any obligation and behind closed doors. i think this is completely against the spirit of this whole process of discharge, which should be open and transparent. we cannot vote for discharge in respect of the committee of the regions either. already a year ago there was an alarm about very serious irregularities in the committee of the regions, not least in the field of travel and a number of other points. it is clear that the committee of the regions has not yet taken this issue seriously. the committee still rejects several of the main conclusions drawn by olaf in its investigation. as olaf expected, the disciplinary processes that should have been initiated have not been put in place. it is quite clear that the financial controller who raised the alarm about the irregularities has been treated badly and has not received the apology he should have received, because he has acted in full accordance with his mandate. as regards the van hulten report, we think it is a very good piece of work. it's high quality, it's thoroughly done, and it's very concrete. as far as parliament itself is concerned, it is time to press for the abolition of the strasbourg meetings. there is no excuse from a budgetary control point of view for spending an extra 200 million euros a year to commute between brussels and strasbourg. it is now, before the end of the igc, that the time has come to put forward this position and we have that opportunity tomorrow. the question of the remuneration of members of parliament will be an issue in this election campaign whether we like it or not, but parliament has itself to blame. it is impossible to justify to the man on the street that we have the different compensation systems that we have and that we have granted them to ourselves. we must instead have a new system of remuneration and wages which is justifiable and transparent. i have therefore tabled, together with freddy blak of the gue/ngl group, a number of amendments to clean up the benefits system. it is important to note that we believe that travellers should be reimbursed for the actual costs of their journey and not for the number of kilometres travelled. we also think that the existing arrangements for taxi services should be reviewed. we have 'free' cars in parliament and there is no need to have a system for reimbursing taxi fares, since you can pay for them yourself from your ticket. we have also put forward some proposals to tighten up the system for when you can get the daily allowances so that it is more closely linked to the meetings you actually attend. finally, i would like to comment on the eurostat deal and also on the commission's statement, although the commission seems to be moving away from it. i think the evidence is overwhelming, as the alarms about the irregularities in eurostat were raised long before the commission decided to act. parliament and the internal audit service were alerted, but the commission acted too late. the question here is one of political responsibility. at the beginning of the commission's term of office the president stressed that political accountability should be demanded of individual commissioners and now that we are in a situation where it should have been demanded, we are told that it is not necessary. this is a major disappointment, given the ambitions of the commission when it took office. we're saying it's not good enough.
mr president, i have expressly put a question to mrs schreyer about the future financing of info-points and carrefour offices. however, even though she has only one other question to answer, she does not want to have a debate on my question. i have contacted our services to ask them why. they tell me that they have been in touch with the commission and the reason is that this is not the responsibility of commissioner schreyer but of mr prodi. the problem is that mr prodi never comes here to answer questions, so i will simply receive a written reply. i would really like to question what the commission says about whose responsibility this is. the info-points problem is caused by the new financial regulation, which is the responsibility of mrs schreyer, and the solution will have to be found in the next budget, which is also the responsibility of mrs schreyer. therefore, it seems to me that this is only a way of avoiding a sensitive debate. that is not acceptable, and i would ask the president of parliament to look into this. i would be very happy if mrs schreyer would comment on this.
mr president, i would like to thank you. today mordechai vanunu is to be released. he is one of the heroes of our time, the man who confirmed that israel has a significant nuclear arsenal that could turn the entire middle east into a nuclear desert. for this achievement in the service of peace, he was rewarded with kidnapping by the state of israel and 18 years in prison. the world largely forgot about him. israel is allowed to have its nuclear weapons in peace while arab and muslim states that seek to protect themselves against the nuclear threat from israel are being bombed to pieces and destroyed. why is the international community not intervening against israel's nuclear weapons? why can't dimona be the same as ossirak? the unbalanced action of the international community is one of the causes of the hopelessness now prevailing in the middle east. a sentiment which was echoed here in parliament yesterday by two of the region's leading peace-lovers, abraham burg and yassir rabo. in le monde, egyptian president mubarak stated that hatred of the united states in the arab world has never been as strong as it is today. the united states is behind israel 100 percent. that's why the united states alone cannot bring peace. there is a need for a better balance, and the eu could achieve this by supporting the palestinians as strongly as the us supports israel. the eu could turn words into action and implement parliament's call to suspend the association agreement with israel. the eu could send protection forces to the palestinian territories. the eu could demand that israel dismantle its nuclear arsenal. the eu could invite vanunu and give him sanctuary in any eu country he wishes. the eu could go even further. a few years ago, i met shimon perez. he suggested that both israel and palestine should be offered eu membership, on condition that they conclude a stable peace. clearly, the eu has an attraction outside its borders that it does not always have among its own member states. let's make the most of it. offer eu membership to palestine and israel as a reward for a final peace.

in writing. - (sv) mr van hultens report is important. we regret, however, that the paragraph calling for the suspension of parliament's activities in strasbourg was not adopted. we believe that all the activities of the european parliament should be moved to brussels. that is why we abstained at the end of the vote.
in writing. - (sv) i voted in favour of the amendments for the following reasons: (1) that the european parliament itself can decide where it will sit (adopted by 275 votes to 223 with 16 abstentions); (2) that the european parliament has one seat instead of three, as it currently has with brussels, strasbourg and luxembourg (adopted by 271 votes to 223 with 21 abstentions), since this division of labour entails an extra expenditure of eur 185 million per year (approximately sek 1 750 million) and after enlargement eur 203 million (approximately sek 1 850 million); (3) that the allowance for costs is to be monitored (adopted by 380 votes to 119); (4) that the allowance for costs is based on the principle of reimbursement of actual costs (partly taken into account in the amendments and adopted by 336 votes to 138 with 28 abstentions); (5) that the general taxation costs are not to be reimbursed and that the allowance for special costs is to be included in the allowance for costs (adopted by 18 votes to 146 with 351 abstentions).
in writing. - (sv) we do not want the commission to put forward proposals which are intended to change the national action systems. we have voted against the wording which encourages the commission to implement the principle of 'one share, one vote', because this is not in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity. such a proposal would have far-reaching consequences for ownership in several member states. different national corporate law systems must be respected. we cannot support the wording which is intended to promote the mobility of workers, since it does not take account of trade union aspects and is not accompanied by a demand that companies take on social responsibility. increased mobility in the european labour market is good, but we cannot accept flexibility at the expense of workers.
in writing. - (sv) what is it? we have voted against the report today for the following reasons: an agreement on takeover bids (concerning the acquisition of companies) has recently been reached. the eu institutions should stick to what is agreed during these negotiations. this report proposes a departure from the agreement. such behaviour may make it even more difficult in the future to conclude agreements if the institutions do not comply with them. we therefore believe that the points relating to the weighting of voting rights and defence interests in various forms should not have been included in the report. when the parts of company law which benefit from harmonisation are amended by european legislation, the principle of subsidiarity must be respected and central management of the company must be avoided as far as possible. the freedom of contract must never be violated in the way that is proposed. we particularly welcome the initiative of industry to increase openness and transparency in management and reporting and hope that these principles will influence future european developments in this field.
in writing. - (sv) what is it? i voted against the resolution (adopted by 422 votes to 30, with 47 abstentions) before the cyprus referendum on 24 april, because the european parliament is thereby contributing to the turkish occupation of the northern part of the island. the resolution clearly supports the annan plan. the european parliament has consistently referred to the un resolutions on cyprus in the past, calling for the end of the occupation and the partition of the country. the occupation forces are being reduced, but they're here indefinitely. turkey retains the right to unilateral intervention and the occupation persists, although it is reduced from 37 to 28.5% of the island. some of the greek cypriot refugees may return to their occupied homes. the remainder are offered financial compensation in the form of bonds, which can be redeemed in 20-25 years at an unknown value. cyprus is not authorised to participate in and assist the eu's security and defence policy on its territory without the approval of the turkish government. in practice, cyprus will not become a full member of the eu. turkish citizens shall have the same rights of entry and residence in cyprus as greek citizens. cyprus cannot be included in schengen cooperation, since the entire population of turkey would have free access to the schengen area. the european parliament calls on the commission to take the necessary steps to ensure that the eu's financial instruments are used effectively and to ensure that the eu's financial instruments are used effectively.
in writing. - (sv) we are voting on the following points: point 13: we welcome increased european cooperation between national coastguards in terms of joint operations, planning and capacity building. we therefore do not think that the creation of a new parallel activity in the form of a european coast guard service is necessary. point 17: we are hesitant about european funding for emergency ports. we believe that the countries which have taken up their responsibilities and financed emergency ports along their coasts should not be burdened by the failure of other countries to establish emergency ports.

mr president, i would like to thank you. commissioner, i have to ask you. i would like to begin by thanking both rapporteurs for their excellent work. i would like to pay special tribute to christa global growth has not picked up the pace we wanted and expected. although the u.s. economy is improving, many problems remain. the double deficit and political uncertainty are draining economic momentum. the growth of the euro area is also not impressive. we are well aware of the figures. as for the eu-15, it appears to have a better economic momentum, with growth of 2% this year and 2.4% next year. the new member states are faring much better, with growth of 3.5% this year and an average of 4% next year. we can only dream of that in the eu-15. we know that the changes in the new member states have been very extensive, and often painful. the development is moving in the right direction, although some objections can be raised and caution is recommended, as the international monetary fund, the imf, has said today. the eu-15 should be able to learn more from the new member states to boost the economy. the competition and the growing market that is now opening up are to everyone's advantage. instead of criticizing countries like slovakia and estonia for lowering their taxes and thereby creating room for growth, we should look at what we can learn from this. it would themselves do not want. of course, the competition will be fierce, it already is. the corporate tax rate in the new member states is lower than in the eu, but there is good competition. the need for increased competition, the completion of the internal market in all its parts and structural reforms for the future. we will not support the additional amendments in the christa randzio-plath report, but we are prepared to support the balanced balance proposed by the rapporteur. mr president, we face enormous challenges in europe, but the opportunities of the new europe are so much greater. peace, mr president, i would like to thank you. we agree that europe is doing very badly. the excellent report by randzio-plath documents how terribly bad things look. this is particularly true of europe, not the whole world. so it must have something to do with specific european conditions. i am one of those who believe that this has to do with the stability pact and the policies linked to it. as long as it's there, it's not gonna do much good. we have low levels of investment and lack of growth and dynamism, which actually means we have a surplus of capital. this capital may then leave europe to find more profitable investment opportunities. we also have a tragic surplus of labour in the form of unemployed people. i do not think we can break this unless we break the stability pact and instead start thinking in terms of public investment in the keynesian tradition of experience. it is forbidden to speak about keynes today, but i think it is extremely important to start discussing keynes and post-keynesianism. the fact that it's not done must be some kind of ideological blockage. i can't see it any other way. it is not much use having a good employment policy as long as those who are trained and qualified are not employed. the companies don't want them. we need to invest in public investment. i believe that this is not just a question of infrastructure, but also, to a large extent, of public service consumption. it needs to be increased. i also believe - another thing that should not be discussed - that it is time to reduce working hours in order to get more people into work. this would improve balance and increase productivity at work. such measures are needed if we are to get anywhere, but as long as neo-liberal policies dominate, europe will continue to suffer, unfortunately.
in writing. - (sv) we support the compromise result in the second railway package, firstly because it will make international rail freight transport faster and easier, for the benefit of the environment and for the transfer of goods from the road to the railway, and secondly because it does not lead to a deregulation of rail passenger traffic. we believe that the consequences of deregulation in sweden must first be examined, as the swedish government is also doing at the moment, before we possibly proceed with further deregulation.
in the case of the new member states, the commission will be able to take account of the fact that the budgetary situation is changing. the budget must be re-prioritized so that less money is spent on agricultural aid. we are also concerned about budgetary discipline and critical of the commission's proposals for increases in expenditure. in this situation we will not comment on the exact levels to be reached by the new budgetary plan, partly because it is difficult to forecast expenditure and the priorities are not defined, and partly because we do not want to limit the new parliament with precise figures.

in writing. - (sv) what is it? the enlargement is a historic event for which we liberals have been working for a long time. we are aware that enlargement will cost money and that it must be financed jointly. of course we must show solidarity with our new member states. before we increase the budget, however, we must look at what can be cut, since the current budget is anything but watertight. we must close these gaps before we require additional funds. when it comes to tax money, it doesn't matter what level of society we're talking about. we must manage the public's trust well. the eu must therefore set a good example by making existing resources more efficient and better used.

in writing. - (sv) i have voted against the conclusion of the present cooperation agreement with pakistan because of the lack of human rights in the country, particularly in the area of religious freedom, where the christian minority lives in difficult circumstances. the pakistani regime has chosen to continue to give the military a great deal of influence in the country, at the expense of more democratic development. the turn of events is disturbing, not least in light of the senate's passage of new constitutional laws that expand the military's influence and make it permanent. furthermore, the regime has chosen to imprison opponents. i think it is absurd that the european parliament should have recommended the present agreement, given that the european union has a great responsibility for promoting human rights in the world and that these values should be the basis for cooperation agreements with third countries.
in the case of the united states, the european union has a duty to ensure that the united states does not violate the human rights of its citizens. in many ways, it's a good analysis with well-formulated conclusions. we believe, however, that the report should have confined itself to the areas of human rights and freedoms enshrined in the un charter. the extension of the report and the introduction of new so-called human rights of the social type weaken the demands on traditional freedoms and human rights and the criticism of countries which do not fulfil them. we are not opposed, for example, to social rights depending on the economic conditions of the country. the situation of the disabled is of course infinitely better in sweden than in poor countries such as bangladesh and laos. on the other hand, there is no reason to condone crimes against human rights and democratic freedoms and rights simply because they are committed in poor countries.

mr president, i would like to thank you. commissioner vitorino, you are in charge. chairman of the board! i think we are all still in the historical frenzy of this weekend's celebration of european unity. it is a privilege to stand here in this chamber with colleagues from 24 other countries. the eu will never be the same again. but after this celebration comes a day in the life, the european union, marked by negotiations, talks and compromises. this approach is far superior to any other in terms of moving european cooperation forward, but it also has its shortcomings and it will not always be easy to agree with so many different countries, wills, cultures, backgrounds and expectations. we also have a great demand and pressure from citizens and expectations for the eu to function and deliver. that is why we need a rulebook; mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to thank mr nielson for his report. chairman of the board! the eldr group in this house has perhaps been the most active in pushing for enlargement. we've been pushing and committing to a date. we have worked closely with the candidate countries, with our sister parties and others. we have invited them here during the sessions. we have been active in the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy and we were the only political group to vote unanimously for each country in the vote on membership here in the house. of course, we rejoice greatly these days when what we have been fighting for has finally become a reality. we have finally united the european continent, which has been divided for so long by war, misery, hostility and walls. now there is no longer an "us" and a "them". now we are all "we": 25 countries, 450 million citizens who have come together to solve common problems, defend common values and build a common future. but this is not the end. the eu will continue to grow. we look forward to welcoming bulgaria and romania in a few years' time. we look forward to the start of the negotiations with croatia, and the eldr group also supports the turkish candidacy. there have been major reforms and changes in turkey under the leadership of recep tayyip erdoğan; the country has been modernised and democratized. there is certainly a lot to be done. the role of the military in politics is not acceptable; there are political prisoners; the kurds are still treated in a degrading and discriminatory manner and the trial of leyla zana leaves much to be desired. we do not grant any exemptions - mr president, i would like to thank you. economic and social policy in the eu is important and the european model is based on a balance between growth, trade union rights, good labour market conditions and sustainable development. we must speed up this policy. for growth, it is about investing in research and development, training of the workforce, investment and so on. equally important is the influence of trade unions on the development of the individual workplace in the form of a better working environment. it is equally important that we make development sustainable in the long term. i'm optimistic about this. i believe that enlargement can be positive, not just for the new countries but for the whole of the eu - also for us who are members of parliament and former members of the eu. i think it could be a success, a win-win situation. i myself come from the baltic sea region and live in sweden. i believe that growth can be boosted by increased trade across the baltic sea. it is important that we do not compete with each other through social dumping, worse working conditions or tax dumping which erodes our welfare systems, but that we maintain a high level of rights in the labour market and in our welfare systems. when we invest, we should do so in new, clean technologies which also create sustainable development and make growth long-term and not just short-term. i am optimistic, but that requires that the balance of the social model be maintained.
madam president, i would like to thank you. in the european parliament elections in june, turnout was very low and eurosceptic parties made significant gains. opinion polls show that public confidence in the eu and its institutions is declining in most member states. the legitimacy of the european union is being progressively undermined. why is that? there are two main explanations. first, the development of the eu means that political power is increasingly being taken away from the citizens. secondly, citizens increasingly feel that eu resources are being wasted in waste and corruption. the new swedish party junilistan, which i represent, has stood for election on both these issues, and we have been given a strong political mandate to oppose the continued transfer of political power from the member states to brussels, and to contribute to an efficient and honest use of european taxpayers' money. the commission must stop entering into new areas all the time. we do not want the working hours of doctors to be regulated at eu level. we do not want hunting policy to be decided at eu level. we do not want a common tax or social policy. the ongoing centralisation and bureaucratisation of political power is in fact a threat to europe's future. europe's remarkable economic, scientific and social successes have been largely the result of an institutional competition between small political entities to find good solutions to such issues. the cradle of europe was ancient greece with its small states. the independent states of northern italy during the renaissance, the flood of german states in the 18th and 19th centuries, provided the great moments in european history. the industrial revolution began in england with a population of only a few tens of millions. the junilists call on the president of the commission to make the new mandate an epoch of political decentralisation and of the efficiency and clean-up of the eu institutions.
