in writing. we shall abstain from voting on this report. we do not regret the fact that the eu budget is in surplus, but welcome the fact that this leads to lower membership fees for eu countries. in order to reduce the flow of money, and in the long term also the eu administration, membership fees should be permanently reduced. we want to see a general review of the financing of the eu budget, which should lead to a uniform system of payments.
i am a new member of parliament representing junilistan. it is the new party in sweden that in the last european parliament elections got 15 percent of the vote. i would like to take this opportunity today to set out the main lines which we believe should form the basis of the eu's fiscal strategy. the overall principle should be that the total budgetary expenditure of the eu should not exceed 1% of the total gdp of the eu countries. we therefore support the position taken by a number of countries, including the swedish government. the expenditure ceiling should not only apply to the next financial year 2005, but also apply in the long term. a credible restrictive spending ceiling could meet the critical public opinion which fears that the eu is growing into an overly dominant superstate. we do not believe that the eu should be involved in far-reaching transfers between countries and regions. experience shows that such transfer schemes have a negative impact on long-term growth. my second point concerns the fact that some countries are now paying far more than we are getting back in the form of grants. the net contribution should therefore not exceed, as proposed, 0,4% of the country's gdp. each country should thus be able to waive its contribution and thereby reduce its overall gross contribution to the eu. such a system would provide considerable administrative relief, as the work on grant applications today draws on large resources that could be used more productively. the third point is that we reject the idea that the eu is acquiring its own resources by imposing taxes or levies on us. the collection of taxes and duties is the sole responsibility of the national parliament. with a strict spending ceiling, own resources cannot allow for increased spending either. the special exceptions that exist must be eliminated. my final point is that the eu must have a budgetary margin within the spending ceiling so that the eu can act quickly in crisis situations. in conclusion, i would like to stress that the eu should not make promises of grants that are not covered by the expenditure ceiling. discussions on enlargement of the union must be based on realistic analyses of the economic consequences of enlargement. new commitments, programmes and pledges of grants may be honoured only in the context of the reallocation of old appropriations. a review of agricultural aid could in the long term create scope for support for the countries with the weakest economies. we will vote on future budget proposals in accordance with these intentions. thank you for your attention.
my colleagues! in sudan, a humanitarian disaster of almost unimaginable proportions is taking place. according to the un world health organization, about 10,000 people die every month in the refugee camps in darfur, western sudan. most of these are children. the world knows what is happening in sudan, but it is still paralysed by this humanitarian tragedy. we agree that the outside world must act now. the question is how best to resolve the conflict. two principles are important to keep in mind. first, broad international coverage. secondly, respect for international law. an intervention in sudan must be based on a un mandate. this will ensure the greatest possible international integration. the un should act in cooperation with the african union. the action will thus be anchored among the african countries, facilitating a lasting solution. the un structure that can be used is, for example, the multinational force shirbrig. this task force was one of the un actors in the conflict between ethiopia and eritrea. the eu's military action risks undermining the prospects for a successful un intervention. the eu's plans to intervene in the sudanese conflict also appear to be a stepping stone towards the creation of an eu army capable of acting without a un mandate. the eu constitution allows the eu to intervene in conflicts without the support of the un. this risks undermining international law. action within the un framework is the best way to create a broad international consensus to resolve the conflict. the action will also be carried out within the framework of international law. the solution to the conflict will thus be more legitimate and more sustainable. thank you for your time.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the debate on iraq has so far often been about the slow pace of normalisation in the country and there has been much criticism from various quarters of developments in iraq. questions such as "is it better now?" have been heard, and one wonders whether the military presence really leads to peace. to those who are wondering, i can ask a question: have you ever been in the middle of a burning war and really wished that someone from outside, the united states, the european union or nato, would come and intervene in your country? i do, and i also have a definite answer to this question: yes, military presence can lead to peace. i represent sweden in the european parliament, but i was born and raised in the balkans, a part of europe that has seen many wars and much suffering. i have experienced the war in bosnia and i know that it is very easy to start a war and that it is very difficult to stop it. it is even harder and takes even longer to rebuild the country, and even longer to restore trust between people. i have a feeling that those who criticize the process in iraq today have never been anywhere near a war. it also reminds me of the saying, "the full understands not the hungry". it is so easy to see obstacles and problems and what can be done better and faster, but the fact is that many of today's iraqi problems are an old legacy of saddam hussein's dictatorship. the fact is that iraq today is a better iraq without saddam. we must make sure that we are involved in helping to make iraq an even better iraq tomorrow. in the case of the balkans, the outside world has understood and accepted that it takes a long time to rebuild a country, both literally and figuratively, but there is very limited understanding that the progress towards peace and democracy in iraq will take time. the eu must now stand up for our values of freedom and unitedly actively support the forces that want to build a new iraq, a sovereign and democratic country that takes its place in the international community. thank you, mr president. the commissioner! it is not the first time that we have spoken about iraq in this house and it is quite natural because events in iraq dominate the international news flow. the reports are disturbing. the terrible violence, the terror against women, children and civilians. mostly iraqis, of course, but also foreign journalists, aid workers, businessmen and military personnel. the abductions are absolutely abhorrent and it is good that we in this house are in agreement one hundred percent at least on our demand that the abducted be released and that the violence must end. the liberal group of course rejects any kind of violence and calls on the iraqi authorities to ensure that those responsible are brought to justice. in this context, we also regret the reintroduction of the death penalty. we are divided on the iraq issue, as are many other countries, in many other parts of the world, and this has meant that we have only been able to agree on a rather watered-down resolution. i still hope we can put the past behind us and look forward. the whole world has a responsibility to support the iraqi people and the development towards a democratic and sovereign iraq that takes its place in the international community. we in the eu can provide support in many ways in cooperation with the un and international bodies. we have a role to play in supporting elections, the economy, building civil society and drafting a democratic constitution. in the future, a un-led multinational force must take responsibility for the security of iraq, and the eu and its neighbours can play an important role in this. of course, the ultimate responsibility lies with the iraqi people, but we all have a responsibility to make this process a success, for the sake of iraq but also for the stability of the whole region. in writing. we believe that the european union's fisheries policy must be reviewed. over-exploitation of fishing waters drives people into ever greater poverty and deprives the marine environment of a vital link in the food chain. it would be better if the countries with which the union has fisheries agreements were instead given assistance so that they could develop their own fisheries. we do not accept amendment no 7 by alde/adle, which provides the commission with an independent source of revenue. we believe that if the union concludes a fisheries agreement with a third country, the total amount of the financial compensation to that third country should be financed by the member states of the union whose fishing vessels are taking advantage of the agreement in question. the member states may then decide whether they will finance this expenditure by charging fees to their fishing vessels.
mr president, i would like to thank you. in modern economies, the budget balance acts as an automatic stabilizer of the real economy. stability in the real economy and hence in employment therefore presupposes instability in the budgetary balance. the stability pact was therefore fundamentally flawed. by emphasising the stability of public finances, it contributed to the instability of employment. the commission's reform plan is therefore a step in the right direction, but it is a cause for concern. this step is taken because large and powerful countries failed to meet the requirements set out in the stability pact. intellectual arguments and political will to stabilise employment were not enough. it does not bode well for continued cooperation within the eu that power is more important than intellectual arguments. it was therefore right, and important, to require that large countries also comply with the rules in force, even when they are less appropriate. the problems are now decreasing for countries with high structural budget deficits such as france and germany, but increasing for countries with high public debt such as italy, greece and belgium.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to start by saying something, let's look at what is really important in the european economy, and that is that we have low inflation and low interest rates in the entire european economy today. it's easy to look back to the 1970s and see what it was like then, because then we had high inflation, high unemployment and governments that were thinning their budget deficits. thanks to the stability and growth pact, we have a common code and a common criterion to counteract this. the paradox is that we have this debate today in part because the stability pact has worked as it should. it has put political pressure on those countries which are increasing their deficits and is therefore in the interest of all of us whether we have the euro or not, whether we are in monetary union or not. the fundamental problem of the european economy, which affects the image of the stability pact, is that too many countries have economies that are not flexible and that are not able to cope with the deficit situation in the worst of times. i would like to say that there is a direct link between the stability pact and the lisbon process, because either we have a flexibility that develops in the national economies of the member states, which gives dynamism and growth, or which gives a more flexible interpretation of the rules, but we do not solve europe's problems by introducing other rules. i would therefore appeal to the commission that whatever the criteria are, they must be objective and clear. the solution is not change, but the reform of the european economy.
at least 10 countries will vote on the constitution. this is wise, since a constitution regulates the power of politicians. it can only be given by the people, not taken from them. i hope my country becomes the 11th country that believes in democracy and lets the people decide in a referendum. the eu institutions must support the people and democracy by being neutral in their communication and objectively pointing out the changes compared to previous treaties. the role of the institutions is not to fix "yes" in referendums, but to give people the basis for decision-making. otherwise, the eu is violating its own democratic ideals. i am glad that the commission will not finance campaigns, but if it does, the yes and no sides must have equal resources.
this debate can be divided into two parts. portugal is reported to have at least 20,000 illegal abortions per year and among europe's highest rates of teenage pregnancy. the midwife maria do ceu, who performed illegal abortions, was sentenced to seven and a half years in prison. the portuguese women are not alone in being denied the right to free abortion, but so are the irish, maltese and polish women. women have the right to their own bodies and the right to sexual and reproductive health is enshrined in the convention on human rights, which all eu countries have ratified. states which have ratified international conventions shall also respect those conventions. the second part of the debate is about the action itself in the case of 'women on waves'. portugal, as a state, pledged to respect the four freedoms of movement when it joined the european union. the exception invoked is national security. i would like to refer to both community law and the convention on the law of the sea. i do not think portugal is such an unstable country that six people on a boat endanger national security. the principle of proportionality cannot therefore be invoked in the present case. finally, portugal has broken its promise to the eu, other member states and european citizens on free movement. i asked members to reflect on what it would look like in europe if other european governments were to consider which groups of citizens do not share the views of those represented by their government and call in the navy to prevent them from entering the country. this is clearly not an acceptable situation. the portuguese government's actions have challenged not only women and citizens throughout europe who want to protect human rights and respect for international conventions, but also the whole of the european idea and the free movement of people in europe. i would therefore urge both the commission and the council to take clear action on this matter without delay. mr president, i would like to thank you. as all colleagues know, women have had the right to vote, to be able to decide for themselves and their property for more than a hundred years. in addition to taking responsibility for their own lives, women have also been responsible for the lives of their children. even though women have been able to legislate and so on for so long, we women are still clearly not able to decide about our own bodies and our own sexuality. the portuguese government's approach to 'women on waves' cannot be interpreted in any other way. the portuguese government's actions not only deprive us women of our rights, but also violate the european parliament's resolution to promote information campaigns on sexual and reproductive rights. i want to not only debate women's rights, but to put children at the centre of this debate. contrary to the views of some debaters and their lack of empathy and understanding, i would like to say that the lives of children are too important to be left to chance alone. children have an unrestricted right to be born welcome and with the conditions for a good life. i am not usually a conspirator, but i cannot help but wonder whether the portuguese government's actions are not largely due to the fact that it is women and women's organizations that are at stake. i'm thinking, if this ship we're debating had been men for violence as a conflict solution, i think that ship would have been allowed to dock. if the council and the commission are serious about the europe of women, then we cannot refrain from reacting to the actions of the portuguese government. now you must stand with the women and children.

mr president, i would like to thank you. i'm going to do a general reflection. the eu now faces the difficult challenge of establishing common legal rules for the enlarged eu and soon for the even more enlarged eu. the requirement of legal certainty must be the same in the eu-30 as in the eu-25. we swedes are expected to hand over a 21-year-old, kalle jonsson, to greece under arrest warrants. kalle jonsson has been waiting for trial since 2001, for 3 years for a young person, is that reasonable? the eu's legal system is based on mutual trust. trust in each other's legal systems. most people in my country think that the eu is about trade and the single market, they have no idea that we should recognise each other's judgments, even in tomorrow's enlarged eu once again. and no matter how, for example, the admission has come. the eu already has and will have more problem areas within its own external borders. at the same time, it is necessary to build the necessary public confidence in the justice system in all member states. it's harder than getting citizens to sign the treaty. if the anchoring is not achieved within the time allotted, then enlargement is delayed or legal cooperation is weakened or even legal certainty is compromised. it's very simple. how can the commission do this, or are we talking about a dream, are we talking about a utopia? finally, thank you very much, mr vitorino, for helping us to move closer to that dream, and thank you very much, mr bourlanges, for showing that you intend to try to speed up this process.

in writing. we have voted against this report. we believe that the common agricultural policy (cap) should be abolished. the enlargement of the union shows that it will be completely unreasonable to continue to pursue the cap in the way it has done so far. it would be unreasonable to drag new member states into a doomed system and to accustom them to the rules and subsidies of that system. we are increasing financial aid to the countries that are to become members of the union, but this must be channelled to the less favoured regions and focused on education, infrastructure and judicial institutions.
in writing. we moderates think it is wrong to support advertising of agricultural products with public funds, both within the eu and in the rest of the world. today, the eu, through the commission, advertises agricultural products such as tulip bulbs and olive oil at a cost of between eur 60 and 70 million annually. competitive products can do without these public funds, products that are not in demand should not be held in such a way with taxpayers' money. in today's vote we are casting our votes as we face a choice between two options, both of which involve continued support for the advertising of agricultural products.
in writing. we voted against this report in the vote. we believe that the common agricultural policy (cap) should be abolished and that agricultural products should be sold on a free market without the eu investing financial resources in information campaigns and sales promotion measures for these products. the eu's financing of promotion measures in third countries is particularly serious, as it constitutes unfair competition with agricultural producers in non-eu countries.
in writing. the undersigned voted in favour of this resolution in the final vote. we did so despite losing parts of the vote on points which we would have liked to have deleted from the resolution. the first concerns paragraph 3, which sets the date for referenda. we do not think that the european parliament should make any proposals on this matter. this also concerns paragraph 6, which calls on the council and the commission to draw up an appropriate campaign and information strategy. we believe that an information strategy is needed, but we do not believe that the council and the commission should engage in campaigning.
in writing. we are voting against the leinen report because we reject the proposal for a constitution or a constitution for the european union. the constitution increases the centrality of the eu and helps to transform it into a form of state with global ambitions. the eu constitution is of crucial importance for the future of europe and for the self-determination of the european peoples. it should therefore be decided by referendum in each eu country, with the 'yes' and 'no' sides given equal resources and the opportunity to participate in the debate on equal terms.
in writing. we have voted against this report because it is not in line with the subsidiarity principle which should be the basis for the construction of the european union. it proposes a european body for victims of terrorism (paragraphs 1b to 5), the creation at european level of a core of operational provisions on judicial and police cooperation (paragraphs 1c to 2), the establishment of a common data protection authority at european level (paragraphs 1c to 8), the strengthening of eurojust with a view to the creation of a european public prosecutor's office (paragraphs 2e to 2), etc. a uniform immigration and asylum policy is also to be established within the eu in a number of areas. we would like to see increased cooperation between member states on these issues for the development of a humane asylum policy, in accordance with the geneva convention, for the whole of europe. we reject, however, the development of a federal legal system, with ever-expanding institutions, to which the above proposals obviously refer.
in writing. we are in favour of the european union not pursuing a foreign policy. discussions on, for example, a peaceful solution in georgia will take place within the framework of the work of the member states in the united nations.
mr president, i would like to thank you. by creating a monetary union with a single currency, 12 of the eu member states have taken major risks with employment. the eu countries are characterised by a weak ability to adapt to various structural changes and are therefore plagued by persistently high unemployment rates. the risks of the euro are already clearly visible, especially in germany. this country has been subjected to an excessively tight monetary policy in recent years, and hundreds of thousands of german workers have thus been unnecessarily forced into unemployment. these problems will grow as the outflow of industrial activity to eastern europe and asia hits different hard in the euro countries over the next five years. unfortunately, the policies of the european central bank have exacerbated these unemployment problems in several member states. like most modern central banks, the european central bank works mainly with an inflation target. this is in line with economic theory, but the target has been chosen too low. the central banks of the united kingdom, sweden, new zealand and the united states have higher inflation targets. modern research suggests that the inflation target should be set at 2.5 or even 3 percent on average over a business cycle so as not to unnecessarily drive people into unemployment. the ecb and its principals should therefore raise their inflation target to at least 2.5% on average and deserve sharp criticism for their inability to align their policies with the way the european mixed economy works.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i think i am the only car worker in this parliament. my previous job was in a car factory, so this debate concerns me a little extra. what we are seeing now is a huge push for change in the european automotive industry. factory against factory, workers against workers. the metalworkers are the ones who pay the price, through a high pace and a depleted work content. i mean, what's happening is, for the most part, unacceptable. it can't be that simple to just lay off thousands of workers. we must ask companies to take their social responsibility. they have a social obligation to those who work in the factories. they must engage in dialogue with the authorities and trade unions to find other solutions, such as reductions in working hours, instead of laying off workers. what we also see is that the regulatory framework is not enough. the need for stronger trade union rights and a strengthening of group trade union cooperation. i think it is good that the commission mentioned demand in its introduction. it is true that demand is too low, but one must also ask: demand for what? with the high price of oil and the great environmental problems, it is quite obvious that what we need most today are cars that run on something other than petrol, such as ethanol. here we can see how the european automobile industry is actually lagging far behind competitors in countries like the united states and brazil, where they are far ahead in developing more environmentally friendly vehicles. it is one of the sins committed by the management of the european automobile industry.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the restructuring of the automotive industry has been going on for a long time. in my own country, sweden, where the automobile industry plays an extremely important role, our large car companies have been bought up by multinational companies. that's the case in trollhättan, where gm now owns the saab factory. for the trollhat, this is a very big deal. mr president, i would like to thank you. it is interesting to note that the social democrats from sweden and germany, where the automobile industry is in trouble, are now leaving it to the eu to have the solutions to what are basically problems of the competitiveness of the national economy. you should not ask what the eu can do for national industrial policy, but what your own national governments can do for european competitiveness. this is a wake-up call to the ability of european industry to compete in the global arena. it is not that we now need a european car industry policy. we have experience from the 1970s when a state policy on shipyards was implemented in a wide range of european countries. it stopped development and it didn't lead to the preservation of jobs, it destroyed capital. what is needed now is a policy that allows european companies to develop their competitiveness. it is absurd that we in the european union should have a competition policy which makes it difficult for european companies to compete globally in a market where competition is global. the competitive situation cannot be assessed on the basis of national submarkets when we are dealing with an industry which requires global competitiveness. we must now ensure that we pursue a policy which strengthens competitiveness. none of the proposals put forward in this debate would lead to more cars being sold or produced in europe. they create false expectations and divert the focus from what needs to be done, namely to promote change and restructuring. above all, we need a lisbon process that makes european businesses competitive and that can bring more businesses to europe. that is what europe needs, not a european car policy.

mr president, i would like to thank you. i represent a party that has entered parliament this year. we do not think we can approve the 2005 budget. we consider this to be too much expenditure; commitments rise to 1.14% of gnp. this budget is to be the basis for future financial planning and we believe it could have been more restrictive. there are many areas where you could have saved. but we have nothing against re-prioritizing within the framework set by the commission and the council, and there are many well-motivated re-prioritizations. we do, however, of course think it is important to restrict agricultural subsidies very much. they represent 55 per cent of the budget, as michaele schreyer said, and that is completely unreasonable. i know that this is not in accordance with the treaty, but our proposal for development is, of course, to cut them down drastically and instead use the money for new initiatives. we argue for a percentage of gdp as our goal. it is not a stop to reform, but a demand for a re-prioritization. we also do not think that the lisbon strategy should be the basis for trying to extract substantial increases in eu spending. we believe, in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, that growth is a largely national issue which is not helped by increased eu spending and taxes. that's not the way to increase growth. therefore, we reject the very basic element of the underlying thought. i shall not go into all the details of the re-prioritization; that is a matter for discussion. we need a much clearer link between those who propose spending and those who are responsible for arranging the financing and getting the taxpayers' money in. it seems to me that parliament is postponing spending without taking the political responsibility to call for additional taxation. in the long run, it is not a good division of labour to have one person proposing expenditure and another person collecting the money. hence the tension.

mr president, i would like to thank you. i would also like to thank the council for its answer to the question. my follow-up question concerns the turkish penal code, which has been recently amended. if you look at this new law, there is a 305 article which still states that it is a crime punishable by imprisonment to talk about the armenian genocide in the media. that means that it is still strictly forbidden to mention the historical fact that hundreds of thousands of armenians were murdered in the last century in what is now turkey. will the council raise this article 305 in its contacts with turkey? what do you think of its contents?
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner schreyer, i would like to thank you for your report. poverty in the world is great; it is not news. it is also not news that women are affected more than men. for many years we have talked about development, aid and solidarity, but in practice it has been about far too little money. instead, it has been about large sums of money that we in the rich part of the world have received at the expense of poor people far away. this is particularly true of the union to which i am, after all, proud to belong. poverty both within and outside the union affects women more than men. in europe and beyond, poverty is particularly widespread among women living in rural areas. yet, producing your own food is a step toward ending poverty. how can we in europe help women in the poorest countries? to me, the answer is simple. in fact, a large proportion of the world's farmers are women and our union has a crucial role to play in the food supply of developing countries. our agricultural policy of subsidies and export subsidies is a direct obstacle to the ability of a family in the developing countries to produce food for sale and to bring products into our part of the world. our policy is to shut them out. by contributing to our own producers, we dump prices on their market. the question of our agricultural policy is a stain on the eu. as you know, the responsibility does not lie with this house. it is no secret that european heads of state are responsible for the fact that about half the eu budget contributes to making the world's poor poorer. this reverse aid policy must come to an end. the commission's president-elect, josé manuel durão barroso, was reprimanded yesterday. the fact that we in this parliament have managed to come together and clearly state that we do not accept a commission based on homophobia and hatred of women is a great victory. we in parliament have the opportunity to do much more. i hope we will also be able to abolish the shameful agricultural policy. only then can we say that the european union is doing something to combat poverty.
mr president, i would like to thank you. poverty is increasing in the union, as has been said by speakers in this debate. is that not reason enough to abandon the neoliberal economic policy with the stability pact and the convergence rules? it has been shown that these policies do not lead to the elimination or reduction of poverty, on the contrary. patriarchal power structures permeate our whole society, in the member states and above all in the european union. the eu's economic policy is definitely not equal. it is largely designed by men and for men. there is no equality in economics, politics, education or health care. nor is poverty equal. women are the hardest hit and most vulnerable to poverty and the consequences of living in poverty. i must admit that it is sometimes quite tiring to hear, year after year, those in power utter all the beautiful words about the objectives of gender equality, of reducing poverty for women, etc., and at the same time to know that in practice very little is done. women's poverty is not a natural law. it doesn't have to be like this. with the right political and economic decisions, the situation can be changed. i would also like to ask why the principle of gender mainstreaming has not yet been implemented.
mr president, i would like to thank you. over half the world's population are women. two thirds of all work is done by women, but only one tenth of all income is earned by women. the beijing declaration on the goals of gender equality, development and peace and a plan of action to achieve these goals was adopted unanimously by 189 countries at the fourth un world conference on women more than ten years ago. eu member states were among these countries. the gap between words and deeds in women's rights is still alarming, with the result that poverty has been feminized in the world. we must invest in women in poor countries and thereby also fulfil the un millennium declaration which states that extreme poverty must be halved by 2015. there is no simple solution. poverty is not solved by money, but by a commitment to human rights and sustainable development. the nobel prize for wangari maathai is an example of what can be done. securing women's rights also secures land, water and livelihood opportunities and local development. we must dare to make demands on the outside world. it is therefore important that women are recognised as full citizens in aid policy, both as donors and as recipients of aid. i want to know how the commissioner is working in this area. almost half the world's population are men, to reverse the reasoning. one third of all work is done by men, but nine tenths of all income is earned by men. this is also the case within the european union, and i do not agree with the commissioner's description. wage discrimination is increasing and unemployment among women is increasing. women are poorer and have poorer health than men in the european union. as we now move towards the lisbon process, i would like to know what the commission is doing to ensure women's rights, to achieve sustainable economic development and to enable women to enjoy the rights they have been granted.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i was going to end with a thought-provoking thought. who gives a shit? who cares that over a billion people live in poverty in the world today, the vast majority of whom are women and girls? who cares that women are the sole breadwinners in one third of the world's households? who cares that women's sexual and economic subordination is the driving force behind the spread of the hiv and aids epidemic? who cares that 57 percent of all children who do not get any basic schooling are girls? who cares that a woman dies every minute from causes related to pregnancy and childbirth? who cares that nearly half of all births in developing countries are without a medically trained person present? who cares that 80 percent of all refugees around the world today are women and children? who cares that women and girls do two-thirds of the world's work for five percent of the income? i just wonder when europe and the world will start to take these matters seriously.
mr president, i would like to thank you. our group, of course, also supports this resolution and the call for a moratorium on the use of this technology. today, we have overwhelming evidence that technology is harmful. for example, there is a clear link between military exercises using this technology and the high number of stranded whales. as mentioned earlier in this debate, there is only one amendment to this excellent resolution. it comes from the other side of this house, the ppe-de group. they want to soften one of the absolute keys by limiting the requirements not to use this technology to only certain geographical areas. this is something that would seriously damage the text. it is also a practical impossibility, since these are species that migrate over vast areas of the sea and cannot be confined to certain areas. it is also a matter of sound waves that themselves can travel hundreds of miles. we will vote for the resolution and against the amendment.

in writing. the junilist rejects parliament's draft budget for 2005. the parliament's proposal would increase eu commitments to 1.24% of the eu countries' expected gnp. we believe that the commitments for 2005 must not exceed the 1.14% proposed by the commission. although payments do not yet exceed 1% of gni, they will eventually rise to the level of commitments in the 2005 budget. this forms the basis of the long-term financial framework. it is therefore important to bring the level down to a lower level as early as 2005. the objective should be to get below the 1% of gni expenditure ceiling. here is a selection of proposed cuts: - we believe that the eu should start cutting agricultural aid as early as 2005. the contribution to european political parties should be abolished altogether. - the contribution to tobacco cultivation should be abolished. it may be up to the individual countries to manage the transition for the farmers concerned. the contribution to information efforts may be reduced because we consider it politically difficult to provide objective information in the run-up to referendums on the constitution. we believe that it is up to the individual countries and their political parties to manage this information. the eu should avoid interfering in the democratic process in the member states.
in writing. the moderate delegation has today cast its vote in the final vote on the resolution. the resolution contains much that we consider to be laudable, but since we voted against several of the proposed increases in expenditure in the vote on the budget figures, it was impossible for us to support the conclusions of the resolution. we cannot support the general approach of raising budgetary expenditure to the levels proposed by the commission. nor can we support the sweeping phrases about more support for agriculture, the structural funds and information measures.
in writing. as the head of the development cooperation budget, i have been hearing about a clearer focus on poverty. the allocation for asia, where the majority of the poor live, has increased by eur 37 million compared with the previous year. funding for hiv and malaria is increasing. a new budget line of eur 10 million is also being set up for vaccination against childhood diseases, etc. i have voted in accordance with the following principles: (1) the cap needs to be reformed. export subsidies should be abolished as soon as possible, because of their serious consequences for developing countries. the aid for the transport of live bovine animals should be abolished. (2) environmental policy should be better integrated into other policies. this is particularly true of agricultural and regional policy. the following table is added: savings should be made in a number of areas. for example, the member states should have the primary responsibility for social dialogue. the following table is added: better information on european policy is needed for citizens. this is particularly true of the debate on the future of the eu and a new constitution. (5) the last but not least, enlargement and new foreign policy initiatives such as afghanistan and iraq, as well as work on global environmental issues, the hiv epidemic, etc., must not mean that the eu's budgetary ceiling is being reduced.
in writing. we do not believe that the eu should provide financial resources to bosnia and herzegovina to facilitate the transition to a market economy. we believe that the eu should have limited powers and that this is not a matter for the union. we are not against financial aid to other countries, but we believe that this type of decision should be taken at national level, or in the context of the un. we particularly question why the eu should support a reform of the financial sector in bosnia and herzegovina.
in writing. we do not believe that the eu should provide financial resources to serbia and montenegro to facilitate the transition to a market economy. we believe that the eu should have limited powers and that this is not a matter for the union. we are not against financial aid to other countries, but we believe that this type of decision should be taken at national level, or in the context of the un. we particularly question why the eu should support the privatisation of banks in serbia and montenegro.
in writing. we have voted in favour of paragraphs 1 and 2 because the use of high-intensity marine echolocation is a cross-border issue. cooperation within the eu framework is therefore justified. we have voted against amendment no 1 for two reasons. firstly, it is difficult to define the areas where sensitive marine species are found. second, the sounds associated with high-intensity marine echolocation spread over large areas. we have voted against the resolution as a whole because we are critical of amendment no 1 and we question whether the commission is the right body to carry out a study on the impact of high-intensity marine pollution on the marine environment.
mr president, i would like to thank you. once again, there are disturbing reports from iran of a 16-year-old girl being publicly hanged for a virginity offense. iran leads the international gang of criminals in executions. it's a management group with very little flattering company. this house always condemns execution, wherever it occurs. we will continue to do so, particularly with regard to minors. it is totally unacceptable and it is contrary to all international conventions. recently, the iranian regime has intensified its crackdown on journalists and dissidents. several journalists have been imprisoned, disappeared, tortured and killed. we must condemn it forcefully and demand that those still alive be released or that their families be informed. the so-called human rights dialogue we have been conducting with iran for several years has failed. we have to admit it. it must either be ended or radically redefined. has it led to anything? no, we can't point to any progress. we owe the iranian people a strong eu commitment to democracy and human rights in iran. if there is no success in this area, it is absolutely impossible for me and for the alde group to envisage any kind of association agreement with iran.
mr president of the council! the commissioner! always good to see you again. the eu's response to the tiananmen square events of june 1989 was the imposition of an arms embargo on china. it was our way of expressing our disgust at what happened to thousands of peaceful student protesters. since then, the world has changed. china has changed, but not the regime. china is the world's largest dictatorship. hundreds of thousands of dissidents are oppressed, imprisoned in labour camps, the country is a world leader in executions, human rights are violated daily, freedom of expression is severely restricted and any opposition is basically banned. the regime does not seem to be changing and we are now discussing whether to lift the embargo, even though there has been no change. what signals does that send to the outside world and to china? china is a great trading opportunity and we should trade with china, but we must not forget who we are dealing with. we cannot let down all the thousands, hundreds of thousands, of democracy activists, political prisoners, the young people who are surfing the internet in secret to find out what is really going on. all these people hope for europe. so we're gonna betray them by selling arms to the regime that oppresses them? we have to have european missiles and submarines directed at the democratic republic of taiwan? no, we have to be consistent. it's our credibility that's at stake, and it's more important than french mirage planes, swedish jas gripen or german submarines. the liberal and democratic group is counting on you, mr president-in-office, to stand up for human rights and to maintain the embargo and to send that message to the council.
for the group. now that the kyoto protocol can enter into force, it is important to look ahead to what we can do after 2012 with climate action. the commitments contained in the kyoto protocol are, as we all know, totally inadequate in the long term. it is also important that we have a discussion with the major developing countries, such as india and china, that they too must accept binding commitments to reduce their emissions in the future, emissions which are increasing very rapidly today. to make that possible, i think it's important to speak in terms of a fair environmental space in the long term, and that the only reasonable long-term allocation of the right to emit is that per capita. that is the reasonable objective in the very long term. the us presidential election is of course a serious setback for international environmental work and the us is likely to continue to act as a global anarchist in the environmental field for the next four years. we have no time to wait for the americans, but we know that the day will come when they will be forced to join this work for both economic and ecological reasons. then it is important that it is a strong and functioning job that they are welcome to. i'd like to take my last few seconds to thank you, margot, for the work you've done in these five years on environmental issues. it is not easy being an environment commissioner in the eu. i am sure there are opponents both outside and sometimes even within the european commission, but i am convinced that you have done this work as well as it is possible to do and for that you deserve all the thanks.
we are already seeing the effects today: glaciers are disappearing, the arctic is melting, we're getting shorter winters, more storms, more natural disasters and environmental refugees. the end of paragraph 2 of the resolution states that in the long term all the world's citizens must have the same emission allowances. this particular sentence makes a huge demand on us to take drastic action and reduce our emissions. we are not only stealing oil from the reserves of future generations, but also from the poor of the earth. when we discuss climate today, we can have a five-year perspective for the next election and avoid making difficult decisions, but the eu - parliament, commission and council - must collectively look beyond the re-election horizon and dare to make decisions that radically reduce emissions. otherwise we are committing crimes against humanity and the earth and risk being judged in the hague court in the autumn of our age, eternally judged by future generations. unless the hague is already under water, of course.

in writing. the report aims to amend the so-called argo programme by making it possible to provide financial support for national projects concerning the eu's external borders. the junilists support the idea of solidarity financing for the border guarding of the outer regions, especially since the enlargement to the east has meant that economically weak member states are facing high costs for this. however, the argo programme is part of the eu's objective of gradually establishing an 'area of freedom, security and justice'. this implies the implementation of a common policy on visa, asylum and immigration measures, something which junilista opposes. these issues are to be dealt with by the member states themselves in their own national parliaments. we therefore vote against the motion.
in writing. there are reasons to question the establishment of new eu bodies to support regions in their economic development. we see the danger that these bodies will become permanent and that this in turn will create a growing, and increasingly costly, bureaucracy. we therefore believe that the european agency for reconstruction should be dismantled by 2008. economic incentives should be used to promote peace and stability. the european union is also committed to the development of a comprehensive and effective policy for the protection of the rights of the child. we therefore support the commission's original letter in this respect.
in writing. we believe that the monitoring of arms exports should be carried out by the member states through their national legislation and through cooperation within the framework of the united nations. we are firmly opposed to the idea of an eu arms export control agency. as regards the question of the arms embargo against the people's republic of china, we have chosen to abstain in the votes on this question in the report. we believe that this is a matter for each of the national parliaments, such as the swedish parliament, to decide. we have also decided to abstain on the final vote on this report because it deals with matters which should be decided by the national parliaments.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the context of the directive. the delegation voted against paragraph 25. we wholeheartedly support the code of conduct, but we are opposed to talking at this stage about legally binding regulations and full harmonisation of member states' policies on arms export controls. we prefer political decisions in this area and want the member states to retain the ultimate decision-making power. we therefore consider that the question of a special tax on arms trade (paragraph 40) does not belong in this report. it is also paradoxical that the more guns are sold, the more the victims benefit. we cannot support this proposal without further information and preparation.
in writing. today the european parliament adopted a report on the european union code of conduct for arms exports. i voted in favour of the report as a whole. the report also calls for the adoption of a code of conduct on arms exports and calls on the council and the member states to maintain an arms embargo against china. the report also deals with the introduction of a special tax on arms exports. the principle of such a tax, the proceeds of which are intended to benefit victims of armed conflict and poverty reduction in general, is commendable. however, in order to be effective, such a tax should be global rather than european. in addition, a tax on arms exports should be seen in the context of other possible global taxes. nor is it clear how the taxation system will be implemented in practice. that is why i have today chosen to abstain on the specific question of a tax on arms exports.
in writing. peace and confidence-building measures in bosnia and herzegovina are highly desirable. however, we object to some of the points made in the motion for a resolution by the committee on foreign affairs. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. we also do not support the statement that 'this operation will further strengthen the collective capacity for planning and managing military operations at eu level' (paragraph 11). the above and the general spirit of the motion for a resolution on the increased military capability of the european union are the reasons why we are voting against this resolution.
in writing. the moderate delegation in the european parliament voted today in favour of the resolution on climate change. we regret, however, that the resolution does not seriously address the question of how we are to manage our future energy supply. the resolution should emphasise that the early phase-out of safe nuclear power by several member states is undeniably leading to increased carbon dioxide emissions. instead, these countries should be encouraged to continue using nuclear energy, an efficient energy source that does not adversely affect climate change. otherwise it will not be possible to meet the requirements of the resolution.
in writing. global climate change is a problem which we believe there are good reasons to address at international level. we are, however, questioning the need for a common approach to the eu's response to the climate change conference to be held in buenos aires. in our view, the individual member states should decide independently on their national position in the run-up to this conference. in addition to this basic position, we question paragraph five of the resolution, which calls on the european parliament to call on those countries which have not yet ratified the kyoto protocol to do so. we believe that each country should decide its position on this protocol at national level. we also oppose the idea that the commission and the member states should emphasize the need for climate protection in the context of dialogues with other countries. we believe that each member state must be allowed to determine its own national position. the european parliament is using an important global environmental conference to extend the supranational dimension of eu cooperation.
in writing. the resolutions tabled by the various political groups contain many well-formulated and well-designed proposals to achieve the goal we all seek: an open, democratic and parliamentary government in cuba. however, we believe that the european parliament is the wrong forum to deal with this issue. the work to achieve a democratic cuba will be conducted within the framework of the united nations and through an exchange with cuba whose scope and structure will be determined by national parliaments around the world. we accept a common trade policy for the eu and therefore call on the union to open negotiations on a trade agreement with cuba, as well as with the other latin american countries.

mr president, i would like to thank you. what we are seeing in eastern europe is a second wave of the struggle for democracy. it is the same as it was 15 years ago, and events will be as decisive for europe and for what europe will look like in 10 to 15 years and beyond as events were 15 years ago. it is crucial that we see the seriousness of the crisis unfolding, where the old dictatorship is trying to steal the new democracy from the people. we must beware of a repetition of a development which could lead to a new balkans. it raises a number of important questions, not least as the vatanen pointed out, about how we will look at russia's own development towards democracy, something that requires consistency and clarity. it raises questions about how we ourselves should be able to respect our own common security and foreign policy and it is a crucial test of how we are able to act. i think it is important that we act not only in the light of the gravity of today's events but also in the light of the worst that can happen in the coming days and months, which makes the situation extremely serious and calls for action now. it is necessary to send a clear message to the russian and ukrainian leadership. our only concern should be with the people of ukraine and with democracy.
for the group. mr president, i would like to thank you. as a member of the ad hoc committee, i abstained from voting on a resolution which i had not been informed of before and which i cannot support. i would like to sum up my position here, which is different from that of the temporary committee. i support the idea of a ceiling on expenditure for the next seven years until 2013. i think it is important to have a ceiling which applies not only to expenditure and payments but also to commitments. i think it is important not only to reject this view, which only six of the twenty-five countries are said to have supported, because these are the major net contributors. it should be stressed that consensus on this issue is required for the adoption of a financial framework and that there is a very strong desire not to place too great a burden on national taxpayers. it is also regrettable that the commission's proposal was based on the old report of the committee on the environment. i think that the 1% target should have been taken as a starting point, but that parliament should have a great deal of influence on the allocation of resources within this framework. in other words, this is not a tactic to shift resources to one area or the other. only in this way can a true prioritisation of resources be achieved. i also support the seven-year plan because it sets the budgetary rules in each country, an activity which is not financed by parliament but by the national parliaments. we are firmly against the introduction of eu taxation, because it would lead to a loss of control over the whole of taxation. i would also like to stress that we do not believe that agriculture should be exempted from the kind of re-designation needed to invest in new areas. such redesigns open up many new initiatives.
, for the group. mr president, i would like to thank you. commissioner, what is it? relations with russia are very important for the eu. russia is our neighbour and its size, history and location enable it to play a very important role and be a constructive partner on the european continent. we have many interests, common problems that we must solve together and our group supports the partnership with russia and wants it to be strengthened, deepened and developed. but it must be said that the russian strategy has not been very successful so far. it's like we're just standing around, trying to get nowhere. russia does not want to accept that eu policy is based on certain values that cannot be negotiated away, that must be seen as a red thread in everything we do, and that cooperation is based on give and take. there is reason to be very concerned about the latest developments in russia. i am thinking of the concentration of power in the kremlin, of the media situation - there is virtually no free media any more - and of the lack of a political solution in chechnya, and, most recently, of putin's support for yanukovych in ukraine and, earlier, for lukashenko in belarus. relations between the eu and russia are at a critical juncture and we cannot describe the last meeting as anything other than a failure, although i really admire and share your optimism, madam commissioner. you mentioned that it was decided to cooperate more closely in a more organised way on human rights and minorities. i hope that this will lead to something more concrete so that it is not just a cosmetic discussion club, but that difficult issues can really be resolved in this forum. it would be interesting to hear if you could go into more detail on this decision.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the new map of europe has been redrawn with this year's enlargement and most of the new member states and those currently negotiating membership have had strong ties to russia in one way or another. sometimes these bonds were formed willingly, sometimes they were unwillingly. russia thus continues to play a major role in the stability of the eu, not least in the area around its eastern borders, but it must not actively side with dictatorships or oppose the development of democracy. if this happens, the eu must say no forcefully. the events surrounding the elections in serbia a few years ago, which resulted in milosevic's resignation, remind me a lot of what is happening in ukraine today. the crisis was resolved peacefully then, and we must ensure that it will be so this time too. but that requires europe to show its willingness to cooperate with russia on the one hand and to stress that russia must not interfere in the internal affairs of other countries, whatever their past history with russia may have been. if russia is serious about being a strategic partner of the eu, it must be clear that the cornerstones of this cooperation must be freedom, mutual respect and the democratic values that eu countries share. i have no specific questions to put to mr waldner, but i would like to stress that neither the eu nor russia can ever have any other interest in a country than the interest of democracy, and that this must be both the starting point and the goal of cooperation between the eu and russia.
for the group. thank you to the commission for a good statement! millions of people in the south are suffering from hiv/aids and, weakened by hiv, fall victim to other diseases such as malaria and tuberculosis. millions of children without parents toil their bodies to grow the food they need. many of them do not have access to any medicines, and almost none of them have medicines adapted for children. exceptions to the trips agreement allow the export of generic medicines, but eu countries are slow to implement them, a delay which leads to suffering and which increases the suffering and the number of deaths every day. today the commission and the council have the chance to show that this waiting is coming to an end. however, improved access to medicines must be combined with increased hiv information and knowledge, ambitious aid policies and fair trade so that these countries can afford economic development that develops trade.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i remember when the hiv epidemic started to develop in the 1980s. even then, forecasts predicted that the epidemic could spread as severely as it does today in parts of the world, not least in africa. but such forecasts were dismissed at the time as mere doomsday prophecies. as a result, the world for too long - too long - turned a blind eye to the severity of the epidemic and did too little to develop effective prevention programmes, including contraceptive counselling, to expand care for the sick and to support research into more effective medicines and an effective vaccine. today, the world has finally woken up, but prevention efforts are still inadequate. if we do not do more than we are doing now to prevent the spread, experts say the epidemic will not reach its biological peak until 2060. i dare not think of the consequences that would be in terms of human suffering and economic and social crisis. let us regard this resolution as a strong call to decision-makers worldwide, and not least to the european commission and the governments of the member states, that we must continue to give even greater priority to the fight against this epidemic in the future. this requires that we allocate significantly more resources, both to our own hiv programme and to the global fund. if we do not, the consequences will be disastrous from the humanitarian, economic and social point of view, as well as from the point of view of security and stability. the commission has been very ambitious in its strategy for combating hiv in recent years, but like bowis and van lancker, i believe we need much more ambitious approaches in the future.
mr president, i would like to thank you. today the european parliament is paying attention to the international aids day. it is important that we give our full support to those affected and that we take our part of the responsibility for public health very seriously. for the first time since the discovery of hiv/aids, more women than men are now infected. in some areas young women are up to two and a half times more likely to be infected than are young men of their age. in some countries, nearly 60 percent of those infected are women. many women and girls are particularly vulnerable to infection because of the risk-taking behaviour of other men and the unequal views of sexuality and sexual rights for men and women in the world. the consequences of the hiv/aids epidemic are far greater for women and children than for men and boys. this year's theme for the united nations agency unaids is 'women, girls, hiv and aids'. when family members become ill or die, it is especially the women and girls who have to care for their relatives. when health systems are overwhelmed or collapse as a result of hiv/aids, it is women and girls who must make up for it with increased care and attention at home. women also do not have the same access to health care or medicines to slow down the disease as men. likewise, old, traditional views, such as the right of men to sex, leave many married women unable to protect themselves from infections brought by their husbands after they have been unfaithful. that is why it is shocking that the vatican today in the newspaper el pais calls aids a moral immunodeficiency. i believe that blaming the sick is both inhumane and unchristian. gender equality and sexual and reproductive health and rights must be shared objectives of all countries of the world, and above all a responsibility of all men. women must be empowered in their negotiating position on condom use. women must be given more rights and better living conditions overall. we're all losers today, but a new hiv drug is now being tested in sweden that, if it works, will be a world sensation. a preventive vaccine is something we all dream of, so let's hope for a positive outcome. mr president, i would like to thank you. the court's annual report is essentially a competent and convincing review of the european communities' accounts. but it is a scandal in itself, as we all know, that for the tenth time the court of auditors is unable to produce a clean audit report. it is therefore clear that the court of auditors does not devote itself to trying to expose fraud and other crimes. that is olaf's job, and there are scandals like this all over the eu too. among eu parliamentarians and officials, there is open talk of scandals that have never been brought to the public's attention and shunned, despite the fact that they are well known to many. we find that the court of auditors cannot approve operations relating to agricultural policy, structural policy, aid and preaccession aid. these are the transactions which are the most dominant in terms of volume and which in turn mean that the vast majority of these payments are beyond the control of the citizens. it also means that the eu should not be involved in paying out money to countries and organisations which are then tasked with spending the money at the whim of the eu authorities. such a system is unlikely to be brought under control. there is really no reason to pursue these activities which cannot be controlled in areas such as agriculture, structural policy and aid. there are reasons for helping poor eu countries but it should be done through unspent transfers. aid to non-eu countries should not be channelled through eu authorities but through un agencies or directly from the member states.

mr president, i would like to thank you. in accordance with rule 168 (2) of the rules of procedure, i ask on behalf of my group that the vote be postponed and the matter be referred back to committee. the reason is rule 35 of the rules of procedure, which deals with the examination of the legal basis. article 18 (3) of the treaty explicitly excludes passports and residence permits from the union's fields of competence. the proposed legal basis of article 62 refers to procedures and standards for member states to carry out controls but not to the competence over passports. i ask for a referral back to committee so that the legal affairs committee can express its views on the legal basis of both this report and the report on residence permits which will be discussed at the next partsession. we also ask that the opinion of the legal service be supplemented, since they themselves admit in paragraph 50 that they do not have the competence to assess whether all measures fall within the framework of the legal basis. the us court of auditors has also written a report which highlights the costs and problems of biometric data, so i also question the effectiveness of the measure as such and wonder whether the council has sufficiently investigated this.

in writing. the moderate delegation in the european parliament voted today in favour of the coelho report because the parliament's views on the text would have greatly improved the original proposal if parliament had had the right to vote. we would like to stress, however, that we have our doubts about the proposal as such. there are significant risks associated with the collection of biometric data of human beings. privacy is threatened by the creation of large databases where data protection can be difficult to maintain in the long term, especially if the possibilities for correcting errors are weak. moreover, there is insufficient evidence that these measures actually protect society against terrorism.
in writing. biometric data can help to make our identity documents more secure by making it more difficult to forge passports. while the right to be unidentified is not a human right, citizens' rights must be protected by clearly defining how, for what purposes and by whom data is used. the creation of a central database at eu level would be contrary to both the principle of proportionality and the principle of purpose and would increase the risk of misuse, as well as of the aggregation and compilation of data in contexts other than those for which they were collected. the council is and shall be the sole decision-making body on the standards and procedures to be followed by the member states when carrying out checks on persons at the external borders of the union. however, the council is obliged by the procedure to consult the european parliament, which is why we have chosen to express our support for the fact that the legal security and privacy aspects are better addressed by the committee's report than by the commission's original proposal.
in writing. we support a free and democratic ukraine and thus the main arguments in the resolution. ukraine is a potential member of the eu and therefore democratic development must be ensured. but we are firmly opposed to the eu being given an increasing role in foreign and security policy. the osce, as the only pan-european security organisation, and as the observer of the ukrainian elections, should have the primary responsibility for mediating the conflict between the government and the opposition. the eu should therefore act within the osce framework.
in writing. the junilista has chosen to vote against the resolution because we do not want to promote 'parliament's priorities' under any circumstances. all the debates, and especially the negotiations for the 2005 budget, have clearly shown that the current parliamentary majority is in favour of a sharp increase in eu spending and thus also of a sharp increase in membership fees for sweden. we support the swedish government and the other five countries which have made it clear in a joint letter that the eu budget, both in terms of payments and commitments, should not exceed 1% of the eu's common gnp. parliament should also be free to review all expenditure, including agricultural subsidies, in order to prioritise aid to the 10 new members. the junilists believe that a strict spending framework and a flexibility instrument within a 1% budget unleash a lot of creativity and stimulate new savings.
in writing. the global fight against hiv/aids is a highly urgent issue that requires a comprehensive international commitment. however, we believe that hiv/aids should not be dealt with in the context of eu cooperation. we believe that the fight against hiv/aids should be carried out by the member states of the union and within the framework of the world health organisation. the junilista is seeking limited eu cooperation. we oppose the union's influence and competence in matters already dealt with by other international organisations.

on behalf of the group. – mr president, the alde group supports the lisbon process as the best way of achieving a competitive european economy and it maintains that sustainable economic development has to be a guiding principle in all future regulatory initiatives. cohesion and social development are necessary, but do not take place in europe today. a knowledge-based society is necessary, but is not a reality in europe today. we have to increase funding for research and development and we also have to do a lot more work on lifelong learning. technical development with regard to environmental concerns is necessary, and this does not take place in europe today – at least not in the way it could and should. fundamental human rights, the rule of law and gender equality are what the european citizen want to achieve: that is the sound basis of economic growth. i therefore urge you, commissioner, not to mix up the fight against terrorism with human rights and the concerns for human rights. furthermore, active participation in the labour market makes a substantial contribution to a decent standard of living, regional development and social cohesion, although investment in education and research, as well as government action in cases of serious market failure, also have a key role to play. each minister of finance in europe today is taken up to the mountain top with all the temptations of undermining the growth and stability pact. sometimes the reaction is the same as that of the famous swedish actress who once said: 'i do not have to be drawn into temptation, i can walk by myself'. the alde group firmly believes that a robust and dynamic economic environment in the european union can only come about if the new commission commits itself to reviewing the stability and growth pact so that it provides a strong bulwark against fiscal irresponsibility, while maintaining the necessary flexibility to ensure that europe maximises its growth potential without endangering price stability. the alde group is fully prepared to cooperate with the commission along this very stony path that we have to walk together, but this must be based on sustainable development.
