in writing. three junilists voted against the report because it is part of the provisions of the common agricultural policy which we want to abolish. the cap needs to be fundamentally reformed and that debate must start as soon as possible. we believe that the eu should start cutting agricultural aid as early as 2005. the commission has also proposed that the council should be able to adopt a decision on the matter by the end of the year.
in writing. the regulation to which the report refers aims to improve social conditions, employment and the completion of a euro-mediterranean free trade area through support measures. however, the establishment of a free trade area is best achieved by the eu abolishing customs duties, subsidies and export refunds. the eu's external assistance and investment policy is a matter for each member state to decide, without going through the eu budget. the junilista can therefore not vote in favour of the report.
in writing. we believe that the countries surrounding the baltic sea should be responsible for the future management of fisheries resources in the baltic sea. we believe that the european communities should not have this competence. the question of the management of the world's fisheries resources will be dealt with, on the one hand, through the united nations and, on the other, in the baltic sea between the baltic sea countries. we have therefore voted against this report.
in writing. we believe that the european convention should be discharged for 2003, as there is no reason to question the financial statements. we are, however, against the lack of representativeness of the convention and its undemocratic working methods. among the more than 100 members of the convention, critics of the eu, women and young people were severely underrepresented. the convention cannot therefore claim to represent the citizens of europe. moreover, the president of the convention, valéry giscard d'estaing, and his presidium shaped politics in their own image. proposals were introduced which are not anchored in the working groups or in the plenary. there were no votes in plenary and giscard himself decided the majorities. we share the analysis of gisela stuart, a british member of the convention's bureau, in her book the making of europe's constitution (fabian society 2003): "the work of the convention was shaped by an irresponsible political elite, who had determined the outcome from the beginning".
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the eu. the junilista group opposes the commission's proposal and considers that the eib should not provide the proposed loan guarantee. the junilista group instead advocates that the countries concerned and benefiting from a loan guarantee should themselves, or by finding other players on the free market, guarantee this loan. according to junilista, this is not in the community interest but risks being a costly failure if loan guarantees are granted without the usual market test. the junilist would like to see the commission's proposal voted against, but finds that their original proposal is less bad than the one presented by the committee on budgets!
in writing. the readmission agreement with sri lanka forms part of the implementation of a common immigration policy. the junilist does not want to be part of a fortress europe. measures relating to illegal immigration and residence, including the return of illegal immigrants, shall be a matter for the national parliaments of the member states. we therefore vote against the report.
in writing. we have voted against this report. we believe that the member states wishing to contribute to the international fund for ireland should do so by deciding in their respective national parliaments to make such a contribution. member states may finance this contribution directly from their respective national budgets and the money should not have to go through the eu budget.
in writing. we do not think it is necessary for the eu to create a uniform portfolio of documents for eu citizens to use when applying for jobs. the eu institutions can of course have a single document portfolio for those seeking employment with them. however, such a document portfolio should not be made an obligatory norm, but rather an area where member states should be able to take decisions themselves.
mr president, i would like to thank you. like josé ignacio salafranca sánchez-neyra, i am somewhat confused by the order of the speeches. i am speaking here as rapporteur for the committee on development's opinion on the budget and should reasonably have been allowed to speak at the beginning. i would like to congratulate mr salvador garriga polledo on a very skilful job, on his great attention to the views of the various committees and on the overall quality of the negotiations. as the representative of the committee on development today, i am quite pleased with the result. by using the flexibility mechanism, we have avoided making drastic cuts in long-term development programmes to make room for iraq. i very much agree with josé ignacio salafranca sánchez-neyra that we must find a long-term solution to the problem that almost every year arises in connection with major disasters and crises. we must protect our long-term development programmes if we are to have a chance of making a constructive contribution to the achievement of the mdgs. as regards the specific focus on category 4, i am very pleased to have heard the call for a clearer poverty targeting of the budget. this will be achieved by increasing the allocation for operations in asia, where the vast majority of the world's poor live, by eur 30 million. this is also done by increasing efforts in the field of health. more resources are being allocated to reducing the spread of hiv, malaria and tuberculosis. in addition, as john bowis has just emphasized, a new programme of eur 10 million is being set up for other poverty-related diseases, with a particular focus on vaccinations of young children. much more resources are needed in the field of health, not least in africa. the increases we are now deciding on mean that we are continuing to work with determination to increase support for the health sector within the aid framework, and that is the right way forward.
madam president, i would like to thank you. the long-term budgetary plan is an important document. it will place a restriction on annual budgets until 2013. it will be decisive for the choice of areas and the level of commitment the eu can undertake over the next 7-8 years. the commission has recently presented its view of the long-term budget. the new commission has taken over the old commission's proposal, the so-called prodi package. that means a massive expansion of spending. for some countries, this is a 50 percent increase in real terms. i do not find this acceptable. i believe that the new commission should have listened more carefully to what the six largest net contributors have said. they have demanded that the budget plan include a spending ceiling of 1 percent of gross national income. there should be a ceiling not only on payments but also on commitments. it is not possible, as the commission proposes, to allow commitments to run out at 1.27% of gnp. it is the ceiling that members have committed themselves to pay. after 2013, there's no way to expand beyond general economic growth unless all countries agree to drop their veto power. i don't think they will. the commission therefore proposes that the eu should introduce some form of eu taxation. we are firmly and strongly against giving the eu its own taxation rights. without control from the individual member states, there is no limit to the eu's ambition to grow into a federal superstate. each member state should let its parliament vote on the eu budget plan before the council of ministers takes its decision, so that it is not done in secret but openly and clearly so that people know what financial commitments they will have. in order to provide the national parliaments with a sound basis for decision-making, the commission should draw up a proposal for an alternative plan which falls within the 1% ceiling of expenditure proposed by us. otherwise, there is a real risk that the commission will ultimately be forced to make unforeseen cuts in expenditure and commitments instead of a well-balanced priority which will make it possible to keep the promises made to the new countries.

commission madam president, ladies and gentlemen, in all areas of activity, the commission is constantly strengthening the protection of the union's financial interests, and in particular the fight against fraud. development aid is not an isolated case and it should be noted that the court of auditors has given a positive statement of assurance on the management of the european development fund for 2003. the risks - and this has been confirmed by the court of auditors and olaf - are not linked to the regularity of transactions handled by the delegation services, but very often to transactions handled by the implementing bodies, whether local associations in developing countries, non-governmental organisations or project management structures. for this reason, the commission introduced in 2003 a much more rigorous approach to simplification of procedures and external audit to allow better monitoring of these transactions. as regards the role of national interests in development aid, tendering procedures are the best guarantee of transparency and competition, and therefore also of reasonable prices. in addition, the european union's development policy is defined by the joint declaration of the council and the commission of november 2000 and by partnership agreements with different regions and countries, such as the cotonou agreement, and, finally, by international commitments such as, for example, the millennium declaration or the monterrey consensus. this is the framework of the union's development policy and it does not concern the specific national interests of any particular member state. this is the comparative advantage of a community-led development policy: it represents european values, principles and objectives, it is perceived by our partners as neutral in relation to national policies and it is based on a real concept and implementation of partnership with these countries.
madam president, i would like to thank you. commissioner, what is it? i would like to hear specific proposals for action. olaf has recently revealed that financial assistance to lesotho was not forthcoming and that the resources were not used for the purpose for which they were intended. i would like to hear more concrete proposals from the commissioner.
mr president, i would like to thank you. here are two very different points of view: one where drugs have been more accepted as a natural part of our daily lives and another where we want to do everything possible to stop the supply and use of all drugs. most swedish citizens, especially parents, are in favour of the latter. it means that drug law enforcement and prevention are given top priority. to say, as giusto catania did, that today's drug policy could be a crime against human rights is completely foreign to us. we don't see taking illegal drugs as a human right. penalties that force addicts to choose treatment over prison are not a violation of human rights. it's a pathway to help you get off drugs. the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction in lisbon also points this out at the end of the introductory summary of its report, which i think giusto catania should read. we don't want to decriminalize drug use. we want to send a clear and unambiguous message to the world that drugs are not acceptable. proposals to provide doctor-led help to take drugs or, even worse, to distribute heroin are equally foreign to us. the key for us is that the content of care and rehabilitation for drug addicts is and remains a national competence. the ppe-de group, like us swedish moderates, will vote against giusto catania's report precisely because we are standing up in the fight against this plague. our path is to break out of drug addiction, not get stuck in it.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the swedish junilista is strongly critical of the report we are discussing today. the proposal calls for a genuine eu drug policy. a common eu drug policy implies a drug-free policy of compromise. in sweden, all handling of drugs is prohibited, except for medical reasons. it is important to us that sweden's restrictive drug policy will continue in the future. we are in favour of prevention, treatment, social training and rehabilitation. the report emphasises a harm reduction policy. we want to work for a drug-free society instead of accepting the use of drugs. we reject the classification of drugs as light and heavy, and the claim that so-called light drugs are not addictive. the lack of internal border controls to prevent drug trafficking between member states is a problem in this context. the eu's member states must be able to control their national borders to prevent drugs from entering the country. internal border controls in the eu are currently virtually non-existent. in conclusion, i would like to say that the drug problem in the eu is largely a consequence of the poverty that exists in many countries in the world. the eu's unsolidarity trade policy of tariffs, licences and quotas is contributing to the global poverty problem. it is important that the un and not the eu work to make it profitable to grow alternative crops in the world's poorest countries.
mr president, i would like to thank you. some of the facts are simple: europe is the world's largest market for hashish. can you stop the drugs? so we just give up and limit the damage? - i know. we know that most people who become addicted to heroin, cocaine and amphetamines start with hashish. that's why it's crucial that we get the recruitment of hash addicts down. according to the report by mr giusto catania, the restrictive policy has failed. the figures from the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction in lisbon, which came a few weeks ago, speak for themselves. in countries like sweden, with a restrictive view of drugs, the percentage of 15-year-olds who have used hashish is 7 percent. in britain, 42.5 percent of all 15-year-old boys have used hashish. the restrictive policy has led to dramatic reductions in the recruitment of drug addicts. according to giusto catania, "light" drugs are not dangerous. according to new data from the observatory, there is an increased risk of a range of physical and mental problems, such as depression, psychosis and schizophrenia. a review of european drug policy is currently under way. even in countries like the netherlands, attitudes have changed over the last ten years. today, only a third of the country's so-called coffee shops remain. the dutch minister of justice has announced that it will probably have to re-examine its policy. there is a good chance of reversing this trend. anyone who wants less drugs in europe should vote against giusto catania's report.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i'd like to warn you about the labels of light and heavy drugs. the talk of soft drugs sends the wrong message. they may seem harmless, but there are no soft drugs. however, these drugs, such as cannabis, are often the gateway to other drugs. i've been to a lot of different drug rehab centers. virtually all addicts have agreed that what is wrongly called soft drugs have been their start in the drug habit that has ruined their lives and robbed them of their future. these users feel anger and frustration that society has not sufficiently warned of the dangers of cannabis, hashish, and similar drugs. in my home country of sweden, we have combined a restrictive drug policy and the criminalisation of drugs with care and treatment for addiction. this combination has been shown to be the best way to curb abuse. the small increase we have seen in sweden in recent years has been due partly to the open borders and partly to influences from the legalisation debate going on elsewhere in the eu.
for the group. mr president, i would like to thank you. the bill we are debating in this house today will have a very important impact on european public health and on the confidence of european citizens in food safety. it is extremely important for all our constituents in our various constituencies, but it is perhaps especially important for parents of allergic children. our amendments are in line with what i and the alde group think is reasonable in order to produce a strong proposal, both from the point of view of competition and consumer protection. i'd like to press a few things. the single market is of the utmost importance. in this single market we want to protect our citizens while setting standards for the testing and processing of food at the points of the chain which are the first contact with the consumer and the first entry into the distribution chain. in that sense, this proposal is a good one. it's also good because it protects important and vulnerable high-risk groups like women, infants and unborn children. we also take a stand on a standard of good agricultural practice. here we have included both integrated control and climate variation to give a more equitable proposal. it is of the utmost importance that these possibilities to take account of local or regional variations do not lower the level of ambition of the proposal. this must be taken into account in the practical application once the proposal has entered into force. it is good that there have been improvements for the food authority, in terms of both time and resources. i also think that the rapporteur has done a very good job. i am glad that we have been able to facilitate the participation of civil society and ngos in the discussion and consideration of these issues.

mr president, i would like to thank you. my party and my political group hope that the european council will decide to open accession negotiations with turkey. it is equally important to point out that turkey has a very long way to go, not least in terms of human rights, the situation of minorities, the cyprus issue and trade union rights. there has been a lot of change in legislation in turkey, but it must be recognised that this is not matched by the reality on the ground and changes in practice. the current turkish legislation also has major shortcomings. i can point to the new penal code which still makes it a crime in practice to mention the genocide of armenians in turkey. it is absolutely unacceptable and embarrassing for turkey to continue to deny this historical fact. recognition of the genocide would open the door to normalization for the armenians in turkey and also to normalization of relations with armenia, which is necessary for turkey's membership of the eu.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the debate on the budgetary plans covers a wide range of issues. the debate on the size of the union's revenue must never obscure the importance of how we prioritise our expenditure. we must give greater priority to expenditure to support the development of the new knowledge society and dare to make smaller contributions to those parts of our economy which are too large and which belong to another era. the lisbon process will of course be the most important element. the commission and the council must not only talk about ambitious targets but also propose concrete measures to increase competition, especially in the sectors of the knowledge society. it is a question of how we will expand competition in education and health care but also in other parts of our society so that we can have the best activities in europe. there is one issue which makes the summit we are now facing particularly historic, and that is the issue of turkey. a 'no' or a delay in this process will of course have different consequences. we would then be leaving the space open to other forces and ideals than those for which the european union stands. the eu's fundamental task is to create security and safety and to bridge differences rather than create new divides. and that means we have to ask ourselves the question: will our security and safety be enhanced with turkey firmly anchored in the fundamental values of the eu and europe? in the same way as our security and safety will be enhanced if it can be extended to the balkans and through the enlargement that has taken place this year? that question is ultimately about the respect we have for the work that the eu is doing. the reason why other countries want to join is the success that the eu has had in strengthening security and safety. this is an important reason why the council must take the historic decision to say yes to opening negotiations.
in writing. the junilists do not see turkey's geographical position or its muslim religion as an obstacle to its membership of the eu. we welcome turkey's move towards the eu. however, we cannot ignore the fact that both turkey and the eu must change before full eu membership is possible. turkey must condemn the genocide of the armenians. turkey must give its minorities full human rights. turkey must achieve a level of economic development such that membership does not trigger tensions that could threaten the single market. the eu must also change. the agricultural policy must be abolished and the structural policy reorganized before the great and poor turkey can be absorbed. in addition, in order to ensure a popular anchoring, membership negotiations should only be opened after the issue has been debated in the member states' election campaigns until 2008. finally, the eu's constitutional issue must be resolved before the eu takes a position on the question of turkish membership negotiations.
in writing. the report on the asylum procedure and protection in regions of origin is part of the establishment of the first stage of a common european asylum system and aims to apply the same procedure for international protection in all member states. the junilist does not want to be part of a fortress europe. a member state of the eu should be able to pursue a generous refugee policy if it so wishes. we cannot therefore support the report.
in writing. this report contains proposals and formulations which are of a narco-liberal nature. we therefore vote against the motion. we are opposed to drugs being classified as 'light' and 'heavy' respectively and to drugs being labelled as 'light' not being addictive. the 'alternative policy' advocated in the report is a more drug-liberal policy, with an emphasis on harm reduction measures against drug use rather than preventive work. we are opposed to a common drug policy in the eu. such a policy would force countries with restrictive drug policies, including sweden, to pursue more liberal and lenient drug policies. it is up to each national parliament in the eu to decide on national drug policy.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the eu has already adopted a number of measures to combat illegal fishing. despite very long transitional periods for important environmental legislation, other legislation has not been implemented. when it has been implemented in some areas, it has been on paper but not in practice. last year we sent a delegation from the environment committee to the ministry of the environment in bucharest. the delegation looked at several of the more controversial projects. we were confirmed in our view that it is urgent to increase the pace of environmental reform. there is a special project which i think the commission should keep an extra eye on during these years. it's about rosia montana, a proposal to build europe's largest gold mine in romania. it's a project that has enormous environmental impacts. among other things, large quantities of cyanide will be used in this sensitive area. the question is whether current eu legislation, such as the provisions on environmental impact assessment and the water directive, will be complied with. the question is also whether it will be able to comply with the new directive on mining waste and its environmental impact. i would urge the commission to look very closely at the rosia montana project to see if it really does manage to meet the eu environmental standards, which i think are minimum standards and something that the local population in the area is also demanding.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the junilists welcome romania and bulgaria as members of the european union, as we welcome turkey, the countries of the western balkans and, eventually, ukraine and hopefully belarus. at the same time, we want to warn of the problems that the eu faces if we take in a large number of members in a very short time - poor and economically underdeveloped countries with little experience of democracy and the rule of law and with major problems of corruption. the eu is designed for economically developed countries with stable democracies and moderate corruption problems. the enlargement of the union to ten countries, which junilistan has been a strong supporter of, is already causing problems. bringing poor countries like romania into an eu that continues to be dragged down by unreasonable agricultural policies and poorly managed structural policies and which itself is struggling to prevent waste and corruption in its own system is dangerous for the future of the european project. it could also prove dangerous for romania. the risk of brain drain is significant. it is also a major disadvantage to enter the eu agricultural system and to be locked into an economic structure with no future. the elections just held are a great success for democracy in romania, but both romania and the eu countries have reasons to wait and not push for too early accession. it may be in the interest of both parties to wait for full membership.

in writing. we support the idea of a flexibility instrument in the eu budget to have a budgetary margin for contingency spending only. the items may then be dealt with in a specific order. they may themselves be of quite different kinds. for example, the peace ii programme is something that has its justification as an internal matter for the eu, while we oppose the eu as an organisation being involved in financing the reconstruction of iraq. such measures are to be decided by the countries themselves, following democratic decisions in their respective countries.
in writing. the resolution also calls for the commission to take the necessary steps to ensure that the budgetary authority is able to meet its obligations under the treaty. we, jan andersson, anna hedh, ewa hedkvist petersen, inger segelström and åsa westlund, do not agree with this. we are not in favour of this. we do not think that there is any reason for them to want to increase payments in the agricultural sector further in relation to the council's proposal.
in writing. the moderate delegation has today cast its vote in the final vote on the resolution. the resolution contains much that we consider to be laudable, but since we voted against several of the proposed increases in expenditure in the vote on the budget figures, it was impossible for us to fully support the conclusions of the resolution. we do not agree with the general criticism of the council for having reduced the size of the budget. this is particularly true of agricultural aid, where we welcome the council's move in the right direction by reducing aid. however, we believe that the agricultural policy needs radical reform and would therefore have preferred further reductions in agricultural aid.
in writing. the junilist rejects parliament's draft budget for 2005. the parliament's proposal would increase the eu's commitment to 1.24% of the eu's expected gnp. we believe that the commitment for 2005 must not exceed the 1.14% proposed by the commission. although payments do not yet exceed 1% of gni, they will eventually rise to the level of commitments in the 2005 budget. this forms the basis of the long-term financial framework. it is therefore important to bring the level down to a lower level as early as 2005. the objective should be to get below the 1% of gni expenditure ceiling. here is a selection of proposed cuts: * we believe that the eu should start cutting agricultural aid as early as 2005* contributions to eu political parties should be abolished altogether.* subsidies to tobacco farming should be abolished it is up to the individual countries to make the transition for the farmers concerned.* the contribution to information efforts may be reduced because we consider it politically difficult to provide objective information in the run-up to referendums on the constitution. we believe that it is up to the individual countries and their political parties to manage this information. the eu should avoid interfering in the democratic process in the member states.
in writing. we believe that the european union's fisheries policy must be reviewed. over-exploitation of fishing waters drives people into ever greater poverty and deprives the marine environment of a vital link in the food chain. it would be better if the countries with which the union has fisheries agreements were instead given assistance so that they could develop their own fisheries. we do not accept amendments which give the commission an independent source of revenue. we believe that if the union concludes a fisheries agreement with a third country, the total amount of the financial compensation to that third country should be financed by the member states of the union whose fishing vessels are taking advantage of the agreement in question. member states shall pay in proportion to the amount of catch they take up. these member states may then decide whether they will finance this expenditure by charging fees to their fishing vessels.
in writing. the measures proposed in the report - to strengthen democracy, human rights and the rule of law in third countries - are much needed and often necessary. however, we are wondering whether this should be implemented by the eu. the un, with its long experience and global reach, is better placed to implement these measures. we are also looking at the large sum of money allocated for 2005-06 for these measures, some 225 million euro. the objectives and actions are not clearly defined or delimited and the experience of the eu's ability to use such money is not good. for these reasons, we vote against the report.
in writing. there is a significant risk that the financial resources provided will be used for administrative purposes and that the efficiency of the coordination of these resources will therefore be unsatisfactory. we are, however, very much in favour of solidarity between different countries in the event of disasters. we do not, however, consider that this is primarily a matter for the eu, but that the respective national parliaments of the member states should decide on the financial resources to be provided in the event of a serious accident in another member state.
in writing. there are some reasonable points in the motion for a resolution. but there are also some proposals which are impossible for us junilist members to vote for. we particularly object to the following four points in the motion for a resolution: the following paragraph is added: stresses the continued need to strengthen the eu's external borders, with the support of the establishment of a european border guard force; the following paragraph is inserted: insists on the establishment of a european agency for fundamental rights, which can effectively monitor the respect of these rights; point 33 of the last part: parliament called for the cfp to be further reformed, including the creation of a european fisheries fund. paragraph 40 states that 'parliament considers that the commission should, without delay, devise an appropriate campaign and communication strategy for presenting the constitution to the citizens of the union in the clearest, fairest and most comprehensible way possible'. we can see that there are different views on what constitutes an 'appropriate approach' in this context. we cannot therefore vote in favour of this motion for a resolution.
